## TASK: Minnesota Family Guide – Daily Data Refresh

**Date range:** Today (run date) through the LAST calendar day of NEXT month (rolling). This always covers "now, the rest of this month, and all of next month." Examples: a run on 2026-07-31 covers July 31 → August 31; a run on 2026-08-01 covers August 1 → September 30.
**Output:** everything is published to the public GitHub repo `avlhohn/msp-family-feed` (branch `main`): 5 human-facing category tables as CSVs (`events.csv`, `parks.csv`, `deals.csv`, `volunteer.csv`, `restaurants.csv`) + 1 raw `msp_family_guide.json` — **the feed Base44 reads (see STEP 6)** — each overwriting one fixed file in place so nothing accumulates. All outputs are also saved locally to `<this session's own working directory>/mnt/Agents and Workflows/` + 1 error/diagnostics log (see STEP 7). **Google Drive is no longer written to for the sheets or the feed** — both the 5 category tables and the feed JSON now live only on GitHub (the retired Drive sheet folder was `1SlAJxG1H-T1-Dnsoy2HYmuOfOTZLmGwX`).

**IMPORTANT — local output path:** Always write local outputs (JSON + 5 CSVs + error_log.csv) into the CURRENT session's own `mnt/Agents and Workflows/` folder. Do not use a path from a previous session — local scratch paths are not shared across sessions/runs; the durable shared destination is now the GitHub repo `avlhohn/msp-family-feed`, where each output overwrites one fixed file. When reusing the "evergreen" parks.csv as a base (see STEP 3), read it from the CURRENT session's `mnt/Agents and Workflows/parks.csv` if present; if this is the very first run in a new session and that file doesn't exist yet, fetch the most recent `parks.csv` from the GitHub repo's raw URL `https://raw.githubusercontent.com/avlhohn/msp-family-feed/main/parks.csv` and use that as the base instead of starting over from a short hardcoded list. Never regenerate the parks list from scratch when a prior list is available — always carry forward + append new discoveries only. The same evergreen carry-forward pattern applies to `error_log.csv` (see STEP 7).

**Note on the companion error-fixer task:** A separate daily scheduled task, `msp-family-guide-error-fixer`, runs shortly after this one. It reads `error_log.csv` and re-attempts open `unresolved_website`/`unresolved_image` rows using the same verification rules, marking them resolved in place (via `resolved_date`/`resolved_by`/`resolution_note` columns) when it finds a confident fix. This task should never populate those 3 resolution columns itself — always leave them blank when logging a new issue; resolving issues is the fixer task's job, not this one's.

## STEP 1 — Determine run parameters

Compute:
- TODAY = today's date (YYYY-MM-DD)
- END_DATE = the LAST calendar day of NEXT month (YYYY-MM-DD). Compute it as: take the first day of the month after next, then subtract one day. (e.g., run on 2026-07-31 → next month is August, month-after-next is September, first day 2026-09-01 minus one day = 2026-08-31; run on 2026-08-01 → 2026-09-30.) This is a rolling window that always spans today through the end of the following month.
- DAY_OF_WEEK = today's day name (Monday, Tuesday, etc.)
- MONTH_YEAR = TODAY's month name + year (e.g., "July 2026") — derive this from the run date, never assume a fixed month
- END_MONTH_YEAR = END_DATE's month name + year (e.g., "September 2026") — because the window now always runs through the end of next month, END_MONTH_YEAR is the month AFTER MONTH_YEAR on nearly every run (the two coincide only on the last day of a month, e.g. a 2026-07-31 run where END_DATE is 2026-08-31 spans July→August)
- RUN_APIFY = **False — PAUSED 2026-08-29, do NOT run the Apify actor on any day, including Monday.** The owner paused the weekly Monday pull until its benefit is proven out, after the 2026-08-29 manual run overshot badly: `maxEvents=250` but the actor extracted 2,048 events (~8×), est. ~$26.62 vs the $5.00 cap (see STEP 4 and error_log `fb_pull_cost_exceeded`). Until this flag is flipped back, the Monday branch below is dormant and STEP 4's Apify pull is skipped entirely — the rest of the pipeline (feed/CVB/library sources, dedup, deals, images, build, publish) runs normally every day. **To re-enable:** change this back to "True only when DAY_OF_WEEK == 'Monday', else False", and FIRST confirm the owner has set the account-level hard monthly overage limit ($0) under Apify Console → Billing → Limits AND lower `APIFY_MAX_EVENTS` sharply, since `maxEvents` is a soft lever that overshoots. Context preserved for when it returns: the pull was reinstated 2026-08-22 after a manual probe confirmed anonymous public Facebook event search works again (retired 2026-08-01 when, on 2026-07-30, every query returned zero events); the value it adds is Facebook-native, no-website family events (neighborhood-org programs, nature-center free days, restaurant kids-eat-free deals, small-town concerts/dances) that the calendar/CVB/library feeds miss; it ran weekly, not daily, because those events change slowly and each run costs money (see the cost cap below).
- RUN_FB_SHADOW = **False** — the read-only Facebook shadow trial (STEP 6.5) is **superseded** by the live weekly pull above and must NOT run. Its filter logic (date-window, MN-geo, personal-host, denylist, net-new dedup) now lives in STEP 4 and folds KEPT items into the feed instead of only logging them. Keeping this False ensures the scraper is called **at most once per Monday run** — running both STEP 4 and STEP 6.5 on the same Monday would call the paid actor twice and double the spend. (STEP 6.5 is retained in this doc for its filter-logic reference only; the `RUN_FB_SHADOW` gate keeps it dormant.)
- **APIFY COST CAP (hard rule — applies to EVERY Apify actor call anywhere in this pipeline, i.e. the live STEP 4 weekly pull and the dormant STEP 6.5):**
  - `APIFY_MAX_USD = 5.00` — no single run may spend more than $5.00 on Apify (raised from $4.00 to $5.00 on 2026-08-22 when the scraper was reinstated as a live weekly feed).
  - `APIFY_EVENT_PRICE_USD = 0.013` — the `apify/facebook-events-scraper` actor is billed **pay-per-event** at $0.013 per extracted event on the pay-as-you-go rate (verified Aug 18, 2026; paid Apify plans are cheaper — $0.010/$0.0085/$0.007 — so always compute the cap at the highest $0.013 rate, which means a cheaper plan can only ever underspend). **Billing is per event the actor EXTRACTS, so our downstream filters do NOT reduce cost — only `maxEvents` does.**
  - `APIFY_MAX_EVENTS = 250` — the `maxEvents` value to pass the actor (set 250 → lowered to 150 → restored to 250 on 2026-08-22 when the query list was expanded to 29 queries across the four suburban rings; 150 would have starved the later queries since `maxEvents` is a single total budget consumed across the whole list). At the billed $0.010/event rate this is **~$2.50** (or ~$3.25 at the conservative $0.013 rate), under the $5.00 `APIFY_MAX_USD` limit. This is a **single TOTAL cap for the whole run across ALL `searchQueries` combined — NOT per query** (per the actor's input schema, `maxEvents` limits total events extracted). Never pass a per-query multiple of this. It is the ONLY per-run lever available through the Apify MCP (see the backstop note below).
  - **⚠️ `maxEvents` is SOFT — the actor can overshoot it.** In the 2026-08-22 probe the actor was passed `maxEvents = 30` but returned **121** events (~4× the cap). If it overshoots 250 by the same factor it could extract ~1,000 events (~$10–13), over the $5 cap. `maxEvents` is therefore a best-effort lever, **not** a hard guarantee — which makes the account-level backstop below **required, not optional**.
  - **Backstop the user must set once (this is the only true hard ceiling reachable):** there is NO hard per-run dollar cap available through this pipeline. Apify's per-run pay-per-event cap (`maxTotalChargeUsd` / ACTOR_MAX_TOTAL_CHARGE_USD) can only be passed as a run-start parameter via the Apify API, and the Cowork→Apify MCP tool does NOT expose it (its schema accepts only `maxEvents`/`searchQueries`/`startUrls`/`waitSecs`) — and for API-initiated runs it is NOT settable in the Console. The real backstop is therefore the **account-level hard MONTHLY usage limit** under **Apify Console → Billing → Limits** (NOT "Usage & billing → spend limit"). Note this limit is the **overage ALLOWED BEYOND the plan's prepaid credits**, not a total-spend figure, and it's per-billing-cycle, not per-run: when reached, Apify starts no new runs (it won't kill one mid-run; expect up to ~10% deviation). The account is on the **$29/month Starter plan** (includes $29 of usage that does NOT roll over; this scraper is the only actor). At `maxEvents = 250` the scraper runs ~$2.50/run × ~4.3 Mondays ≈ **~$11/month**, comfortably inside the prepaid $29. **Recommended setting: hard overage limit = $0**, which caps total monthly spend at the $29 prepaid — a freak overshoot month would just pause new runs until the next cycle rather than bill overage. Until this is set, treat spend as bounded only by the soft `maxEvents` lever.

## STEP 2 — Search for events (run ALL queries in parallel batches)

Search for family-friendly events happening between TODAY and END_DATE across ALL of Minnesota. Tag each event with its city/region. Target 200+ events total (the higher target reflects the sports and paid-activity sources added below, plus the west/east/north/south suburban-metro coverage — these directional passes must ADD suburban results, not crowd out the core Minneapolis/St. Paul coverage).

**Sports and paid activities are in scope and belong in the `events` category** (do NOT create new sheets/CSVs for them — keep the 5-category output contract intact so the Base44 feed and the error-fixer task are unaffected). A youth-league sign-up, a Twins game, a Community Ed pottery class, and a summer camp are all "events/activities" — capture them as events, price them using the existing `price_type`/`price` fields, and tag them (see STEP 3 tags) so downstream consumers can filter. For recurring/seasonal items (leagues, classes, camps) that don't have a single date, put the session/registration window or start date in `date`, note recurrence in `description`, and put any registration deadline/cost detail in `description` as well.

**Age-band keyword variation (do this across the searches below):** don't rely only on the generic word "kids." The family/kids queries systematically under-surface the youngest and oldest bands, so vary the age keyword — run key searches with "babies"/"infant", "toddler", "preschool"/"pre-K", and "teens"/"teen" in place of (or in addition to) "kids." This pulls age-specific programming that a generic "kids" search buries — e.g. baby story time, toddler open gym, teen night. Tag results with the matching age-group tag (see STEP 3).

**Search for venues/establishments, not just dated events (important — this is how standing places get caught):** some of the best family destinations are *places*, not events on a calendar — an indoor play café like Rebe's Play Cafe (St. Paul) offers open play any day plus recurring story times and classes, so it never shows up under "events happening on [date]." Use the establishment search cluster below to catch the place itself, then capture BOTH the venue (route it to the right category — a play café is a café with a play area, so it belongs in `restaurants` with `family-dining`/`play-area` tags) AND its scheduled programming (open-play sessions, story times, classes → `events`, with recurrence noted in `description`).

**Month/year substitution (required — do NOT search a stale month):** The query list below is a template written with a literal "July 2026" and a "[DAY_OF_WEEK]" placeholder. Before running the searches, replace every literal "July 2026" with the current MONTH_YEAR from STEP 1, and replace "[DAY_OF_WEEK]" with DAY_OF_WEEK. **The window now almost always spans two months** (MONTH_YEAR != END_MONTH_YEAR, since END_DATE is the last day of next month), so run each date-bearing query a SECOND time with END_MONTH_YEAR as well, so next-month events aren't missed — e.g. a run on 2026-07-31 must search both "July 2026" and "August 2026" variants; a run on 2026-08-01 must search both "August 2026" and "September 2026" variants. On the rare run where MONTH_YEAR == END_MONTH_YEAR (only on the last day of a month), a single pass is enough. Never issue a query containing a month that the calendar has already moved past.

**`[metro]` placeholder expansion (required — applies to every "[metro] ..." query anywhere below):** wherever a query template contains "[metro]", run it once for each of these metros: **Minneapolis, St. Paul, each outstate metro (Rochester, St. Cloud, Mankato, Moorhead, Brainerd, Bemidji, Winona, Alexandria, Duluth), AND one anchor city per suburban quadrant — West: Maple Grove, East: Woodbury, North: Blaine, South: Bloomington** (plus any extra suburb the user has named for that direction). The four quadrant anchors ensure the dining, community-education, sports, play-café, and community-event loops reach the ring suburbs, not just the two core cities. Tag each result with the specific city you searched, never a generic "Twin Cities."

### Targeted chain family-night check (Chick-fil-A and similar — DATED events only):
Some family-restaurant chains host recurring **Family Nights** (games, raffles, the Chick-fil-A Cow mascot) and seasonal **Daddy-Daughter Date Nights**, but there is NO central calendar — each franchise operator posts sporadically to its OWN Eventbrite or Facebook page, usually only a few weeks ahead, so a generic "Chick-fil-A Minnesota events" search mostly returns other states. Do a quick, targeted pass on the specific Minnesota locations below, then apply the accuracy guard.
- Run, for each named location: "Chick-fil-A [CITY] MN family night [MONTH_YEAR]" and (if the window includes it) "Chick-fil-A [CITY] MN daddy daughter date night [MONTH_YEAR]"; also try fetching the operator's Eventbrite organizer page or Facebook events when a search surfaces one. MN locations to check: **Maple Grove** (11820 Fountains Way, 55369), **Blaine** (595 Northtown Dr NE, 55434), **Woodbury**, **Eagan**, **Burnsville**, **Bloomington**, **Minnetonka/Plymouth (west metro)**, **Rochester**, **St. Cloud**, and **Duluth** (plus any extra location the user names).
- **Accuracy guard (required):** only create an `events` row when you can confirm BOTH a specific calendar **date within the TODAY→END_DATE window** AND the specific MN location hosting it. Put the venue's street address in `address`, the date in `date`, the time in `time`, tag `family-dining`, `family-night` (or `daddy-daughter`), the city, and the metro direction. If no dated, in-window event is confirmed for a location, add NOTHING for it — do NOT emit an undated "come to Family Night" placeholder, and never guess a date. These events are sporadic and seasonal (daddy-daughter nights cluster in Feb), so most runs will legitimately find none — that is the correct result, not a failure.

### Family dining & kid-experience restaurants (statewide — tag with city where applicable):
The goal here is to broaden the **restaurants** category beyond deal-driven listings to capture places that are a *destination* for families because of what kids can do there — not just where they're tolerated. Search for the experience angle, then record any deal the venue also offers using the existing `has_deal`/`deal_description`/`deal_expiry` fields on the SAME restaurant row (a place can be both an experience and a deal). Run these for the Twin Cities and each outstate metro (Duluth, Rochester, St. Cloud, Mankato, Moorhead, Brainerd, Bemidji, Winona, Alexandria):
- "[metro] kid friendly restaurants 2026" and "[metro] child friendly restaurants 2026" (run BOTH phrasings — many roundups use one or the other, so searching both surfaces different lists)
- "[metro] best family restaurants 2026" and "[metro] family friendly restaurants with kids menu 2026" and "[metro] family friendly restaurants with children's menu 2026" (run BOTH the "kids menu" and "children's menu" phrasings — like the kid-friendly/child-friendly pair above, dining roundups and restaurant pages use one or the other, so searching both surfaces different lists)
- "[metro] restaurants good with kids / good for toddlers 2026" and "[metro] where to eat out with kids 2026"
- "[metro] family friendly restaurants with kids play area 2026"
- "[metro] restaurants with arcade or games for kids 2026"
- **NO BREWERIES FROM SCRAPES — EXCLUSION RULE (applies everywhere — this section, the STEP 4 build, and every search pass):** Do NOT search for, scrape, or add breweries, taprooms, brewpubs, beer halls, wineries, cideries, distilleries, or any other alcohol-first venue to the guide. Do not run brewery/taproom queries at all. If a broad search or a Facebook result surfaces any such venue, DROP it in the STEP 4.3 pass — even if it looks family-friendly or has a kids' menu. **The ONLY exception is the three hand-vetted brewery-family SEEDS, which are curated inclusions and stay by name: Fitger's Brewhouse (Duluth), Granite City Food & Brewery (Maple Grove), and Tamarack Tap Room (Woodbury).** Those three keep their seed rows; nothing else in this venue class is ever added.
- "[metro] themed or character dining kids Minnesota 2026"
- "[metro] mini golf go-kart family entertainment center food 2026"
- "[metro] best ice cream dessert spots for families 2026"
- "[metro] diner / pizza place / burger joint kid friendly 2026" (the everyday casual sit-down spots — pizzerias, diners, malt shops, burger joints — are the workhorse family restaurants these searches most often under-surface)
- "Minnesota kids eat free restaurants [DAY_OF_WEEK] 2026" (feeds both this category's deal fields and meal_deals)
- Local family roundups/blogs (Twin Cities parenting guides, Visit-[city] dining pages) are excellent aggregators for these — fetch via `WebFetch` per the web-content policy. In the `description`, spell out the specific kid draw (play structure, arcade, patio, kids' menu, etc.); in `tags`, add the matching dining tags below.

### Meal-deal & small-venue/café special aggregators (feeds meal_deals + restaurant deal fields):
Small cafés and neighborhood spots rarely surface in generic "kids eat free" web searches — their deals live on hyperlocal parenting blogs, deal aggregators, and their own social pages. Mine these dedicated sources every run to broaden meal_deals beyond the big-chain listings. Fetch each via `WebFetch` only (never bash/curl/requests), per the web-content policy:
- **RETIRED (2026-08-28): Twin Cities Frugal Mom — do NOT fetch `twincitiesfrugalmom.com` (either the kids-eat-free list or the discount guide).** Retired as a source at the owner's request: her roundups carried deals EXCLUSIVE to her (branded discount codes like `TCFM` / `FRUGALMOM25` / `TCFMB`) that must not be republished. The publicly-true kids-eat-free venue facts already captured remain in `standing_deals.csv` and keep being applied via the STEP 4.35 overlay; source any NEW kids-eat-free deals from each venue's own site/social directly, never from her list.
- **DEAD END: upparent.com returns 403 to `WebFetch` — do not retry it.** (Probed 2026-08-22. It had been listed here as a live source on the strength of the URL alone, without anyone ever fetching it.)
- **kidseatfree.io** and **MomsWhoSave kids-eat-free** pages — national deal databases; filter to MN/Twin Cities entries and record the day/venue/terms.
- **Macaroni KID** — the per-city editions (Macaroni KID Twin Cities, St. Paul, Minneapolis-area, and each outstate metro) publish local dining deals and family-night specials; fetch each city edition.
- **Each metro's CVB/tourism dining pages** (Visit Saint Paul, Meet Minneapolis, Visit Duluth, Experience Rochester, etc.) and the local **Chamber of Commerce dining/member directories** — good for independent-restaurant specials the aggregators miss.
- For each deal captured, record the specific day, venue, and terms in `deal_description`, set `deal_expiry` where stated, and include the venue `website`. If the venue is a family destination in its own right, also mirror the deal onto its `restaurants` row via `has_deal`/`deal_description`/`deal_expiry`.
- NOTE: Family Fun Twin Cities (familyfuntwincities.com) is JavaScript-rendered and cannot be extracted by `WebFetch` — do not rely on it; use the sources above instead.

### Deals & discounts — free/discounted meals, treats & activities (feeds meal_deals + has_deal/deal_description/deal_expiry on events, restaurants, and parks & attractions):
The "kids eat free" searches above catch the classic weeknight restaurant promos, but a lot of family savings live outside that phrase — discounted (not just free) meals, dessert/treat freebies, and reduced-price admission to museums, zoos, parks, and activities. Run this cluster every run to widen the deal net. Expand `[metro]` per the line-52 rule, and substitute the current `[MONTH_YEAR]`/`[DAY_OF_WEEK]`.

> **DATE-WINDOW CARVE-OUT (adopted 2026-08-22 — read before running part (b)).**
> Part (a) below is dated and takes the normal two-month window. **Part (b) does NOT.**
> Part (b) is almost entirely *standing programs with no date* — Como Zoo is always free, Mia
> is always free, smARTpass and Museums for All run year-round. Applying the two-month window
> to them discards the entire payload, which is exactly what happened before this carve-out:
> 0 park deals of 815 rows and 9 event deals of 2,181. **Standing deals are exempt from the
> date window**, following the same pattern as the "standing/recurring attraction" seed rows.
> A blank `deal_expiry` means "runs indefinitely" and is VALID DATA — never warn on it and
> never age the row out. Only set it when the source states an actual end date.
>
> See `Agents and Workflows/deal-source-registry.md` for the verified source list, the
> corrected URLs, and the recorded dead ends. Do not rediscover these ad hoc.

**(a) Free & discounted meals + treats (feeds meal_deals; mirror onto a `restaurants` row where the venue is a destination):**
- "[metro] kids meal deals family dinner specials [DAY_OF_WEEK] 2026" (catches discounted — not only free — kids' meals)
- "[metro] kids eat cheap or discounted [DAY_OF_WEEK] 2026"
- "[metro] family meal deal bundle restaurant special 2026"
- "[metro] free or discounted kids treat ice cream dessert deal 2026"
- "[metro] kids eat free breakfast weekend brunch 2026" (weekend-daytime deals the weeknight searches miss)
- "Minnesota kids birthday freebies free dessert restaurants 2026"

**(b) Discounted & free events, venues & activities (feeds meal_deals-adjacent deal fields; set `has_deal`/`deal_description`/`deal_expiry` on the matching `events` or `parks & attractions` row):**
- **MELSA smARTpass** (smartpass.melsa.org) — current Twin Cities library-card program for free/discounted museum & arts tickets (successor to the defunct Museum Adventure Pass). Fetch via `WebFetch`; record **every partner venue**, not just ones with a dated offer — smARTpass runs year-round and has no date window to filter by. (This line used to say "available in the date window," which is the carve-out bug in miniature: it told the run to filter an undated program by date, i.e. to discard all of it.) ~45 partners; the family-relevant ones are Children's Theatre Company, Stages Theatre, Circus Juventas, Mia, the American Swedish Institute, Hennepin History Museum, the Walker and Park Square Theatre.
- **Museums for All** — $0–$5 admission with EBT/SNAP at 1,500+ museums incl. Science Museum of MN, MNHS sites, Walker, ASI, Bell Museum, TMORA. **DEAD END: museums4all.org returns 403 to `WebFetch` — do not retry it.** The per-venue MN Museums-for-All participation already captured (Science Museum $3, the MNHS income-qualifying rates, etc.) is preserved in `standing_deals.csv`; source any NEW venues from **Second Harvest** (2harvest.org) SNAP/EBT discount master page and the venues' own admission pages — NOT from Twin Cities Frugal Mom, which was retired as a source 2026-08-28.
- **Kids Bowl Free** (kidsbowlfree.com) — 2 free games/day at participating MN lanes; capture the participating locations and season dates.
- **Marcus Theatres "Kids Dream"** summer film series — ~$3 movies at Duluth, Oakdale, Rochester, Rosemount, St. Louis Park, Shakopee, Waite Park; capture the schedule when in the date window.
- "Minnesota free museum days [MONTH_YEAR]" / "[metro] free admission days zoo museum aquarium 2026"
- **MN DNR free entrance days** — canonical page is `dnr.state.mn.us/state_parks/free-entrance-days-minnesota-state-parks.html` (hyphens; the older `free_park_days.html` is a 404). Four dates a year, one per season — **2026: Jan 19, Apr 25, Jun 13, Nov 27.** Most runs will find none in window; that zero is CORRECT, not a failure. Also "Minnesota DNR free fishing weekend 2026".

**(c) Deal & discount aggregator master lists (mine each run, `WebFetch` only):**
- **Thrifty Minnesota** (thriftyminnesota.com) — statewide free/discounted family-activity and dining roundups. Static HTML, fetches cleanly.
- **RETIRED (2026-08-28): Twin Cities Frugal Mom — discount guide.** Do NOT fetch `twincitiesfrugalmom.com`. Retired at the owner's request because her roundup carried deals EXCLUSIVE to her (branded discount codes `TCFM` / `FRUGALMOM25` / `TCFMB`) that must not be republished in this app. The ~30 publicly-true venue standing discounts already gathered from it (Como Zoo free, Mia free, Museums-for-All rates, the MNHS sites…) remain in `standing_deals.csv` and keep being applied via the STEP 4.35 overlay; source any NEW venue discounts from the venue or its CVB directly, never from her guide.

**Routing — a standing deal is an ATTRIBUTE OF A VENUE, not a row of its own.**
Do NOT create a `meal_deals` row for "Como Zoo is free." Instead:
1. Join the deal onto rows that already exist, by normalized venue name (casefold, strip
   punctuation, substring match on `title` then `address`).
2. Stamp `has_deal=true` and put the exact terms in `deal_description`. Set `deal_expiry`
   ONLY if the source states an end date — otherwise leave it blank, meaning "indefinite".
3. Never clobber a more specific row-level deal that STEP 2 already found.
4. Only if nothing matches AND the deal is food, create a `meal_deals` row. If nothing
   matches and it is an attraction, log it as a missing seed venue rather than inventing a row.

This join is implemented as **STEP 4.35** — run `deals_overlay.py` against the compiled
dataset, using the verified `standing_deals.csv` table. It is an overlay applied after
compile, like the image overlay, precisely because these deals have no date and therefore
cannot survive STEP 2. Apply the MN-geo guard as normal; do NOT apply the date window.

#### STEP 4.35 — the exact nightly procedure

Run this after compile and before the image ladder. It is four commands and two fetches;
do not improvise around it.

**1. Refresh the standing-deal table.** `WebFetch` these two URLs every run. They are the
verified set — do not substitute, and do not re-probe the dead ends recorded in
`Agents and Workflows/deal-source-registry.md`.

| # | URL | Extract |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | `smartpass.melsa.org` | Every partner venue name. Terms are uniform: free or discounted admission for up to two people with a participating Twin Cities library card. |
| 2 | `thriftyminnesota.com` | Free/discounted family activities and standing admission deals; also mirrors the DNR free-park-day dates in an easier-to-parse form. |

**Twin Cities Frugal Mom (both `twincitiesfrugalmom.com` URLs) was REMOVED from this set on 2026-08-28** (see `deal-source-registry.md`) because it carried deals exclusive to her; the venue facts already gathered from it stay in `standing_deals.csv`. Do not re-add it.

**2. Merge into `standing_deals.csv`**, columns
`venue_match,deal_description,deal_expiry,source_url,verified_date`. Merge, never overwrite —
the file is a curated accumulation and a bad fetch must not be able to empty it.
- `venue_match` is the venue's plain name. The matcher is token-subset, so
  "Oliver Kelley Farm" finds "Oliver H. Kelley Farm" — but write the fullest name you have.
- `deal_expiry` **stays blank unless the source states a real end date.** Blank means "runs
  indefinitely" and is correct, expected, and must never be warned on or aged out.
- `source_url` must be one of the two URLs above, verbatim (or, for a venue fact already in the
  table, its existing verbatim source). `deals_overlay.py` derives the
  `deal_source` provenance key from it by substring, and STEP 7's yield check attributes by
  that key — a URL typed loosely produces an unattributable row that reads as a source
  failure.

**3. Apply and check:**
```bash
python3 deals_overlay.py _compiled_work.json      # joins by venue name, stamps has_deal
python3 deals_yield.py  _compiled_work.json       # per-source yield, feeds STEP 7
```

**4. Read the two reports before moving on.**
- `_deals_overlay_report.json` → `unmatched` lists venues with a verified deal and no row to
  attach it to. Each one is a **missing seed venue**, not a bad deal. Add it to the
  must-include list with a Nominatim-verified coordinate; never invent a row for it. This is
  how the eleven MNHS/arts/MOA venues seeded on 2026-08-22 were found.
- `_deals_yield_report.json` → any year-round source reporting **0** is a real failure and
  must appear in the STEP 7 error log. A *seasonal* source reporting 0 out of season is
  correct — the script already knows which is which.

**5. Strip retired Twin Cities Frugal Mom exclusive codes (`frugal_scrub.py`) — REQUIRED.**
```bash
python3 frugal_scrub.py _compiled_work.json      # scrub deal_description across ALL categories
```
Twin Cities Frugal Mom was retired 2026-08-28 because her roundups carried discount codes
EXCLUSIVE to her — `TCFM`, `TCFMB$2OFF`, `FRUGALMOM25`, and "mention the Twin Cities Frugal
Mom post" asks — that must not be republished. Retiring the source stopped new pulls, but the
codes were already baked into the `deal_description` of ~20 **event and restaurant** rows (NOT
`deals.csv` — that scan came back clean and hid the problem), and the carried base preserves
them every run, so they resurfaced in the live app on 2026-08-29. This pass is the standing
guard. It works clause-by-clause on the semicolon-delimited `deal_description`: it drops ONLY
the offending clause and keeps every other publicly-true discount (Children's Museum keeps its
First Free Sundays / MELSA smARTpass / Play for All; the Twins keep student/military/senior
deals; the Works Museum keeps member/military/Gateway). When the code WAS the whole deal (Zero
Gravity, Hyderabad Grill), it blanks `deal_description`/`deal_expiry` and clears `has_deal` so
no empty card ships. It matches on `TCFM`/`FRUGALMOM`/`frugal mom` tokens, **never bare
"frugal"**, so the Renaissance Festival's own "Frugal Friday" promotion is deliberately kept.
Idempotent (a warm re-run scrubs 0) and `--dry-run` reports without writing. Run it AFTER the
overlay/yield checks so it also catches any code the overlay just re-stamped from
`standing_deals.csv`.

**Two failure modes that are silent, and both have bitten this pipeline:**
- **A zero must be attributable.** Before 2026-08-22 the entire deal cluster emitted no error
  rows at all, so "the source returned nothing" and "the step never ran" looked identical for
  months. Every source must produce a yield line, including zeroes.
- **The overlay must refresh its own prior output.** It identifies rows it previously stamped
  by their `deal_source` value and re-stamps them; without that it no-ops on every re-run
  after the first and edits to `standing_deals.csv` never take effect. Do not "fix" that
  carve-out by making the overlay skip already-stamped rows — that is the bug, not the guard.
  (The image overlay hit the identical trap with `curated_category`.)

### Community & special-interest events (statewide — tag with city where applicable):
IMPORTANT — these are events the family/kids-keyed searches above systematically MISS, because organizers don't tag them as "family" or "kids" events even though they're excellent family outings (a car show is the canonical example). Do NOT append "family" or "kids" to these queries — that's exactly what filters them out. Search for the event type directly, run for the Twin Cities and each outstate metro (Duluth, Rochester, St. Cloud, Mankato, Moorhead, Brainerd, Bemidji, Winona, Alexandria):
- "[metro] car show July 2026" and "[metro] classic car cruise night summer 2026" (weekly all-summer cruise nights at drive-ins, breweries, and Main Streets are common — capture the recurring series with its weekly day/time in `description`)
- "[metro] Cars and Coffee 2026" / "[metro] car swap meet 2026"
- "[metro] touch-a-truck event 2026"
- "Minnesota air show 2026 schedule" / "[metro] boat show / RV show / sportsmen show 2026"
- "[metro] tractor pull / demolition derby / county fair grandstand 2026"
- "[metro] parade summer 2026" / "[metro] community festival / days celebration 2026" (town summer festivals — e.g. "-Fest" / "-Days" events)
- "[metro] craft fair / vendor fair / maker market 2026" / "[metro] food truck rally 2026"
- "Minnesota hot air balloon festival 2026" and other regional signature events in the date window
Fold all of these into the existing `events` category and tag with the matching community tags below (`car-show`, `parade`, `air-show`, `community-fest`, `festival`, `county fair`, etc.). Price via `price_type`/`price` (many are free; some charge admission or per-car entry).

### Cultural, heritage & multicultural events (statewide — tag with community + city):
IMPORTANT — like the community events above, these are systematically MISSED by the family/kids-keyed searches: organizers of a powwow, a Hmong New Year, or a heritage festival rarely tag the event "family" or "kids" even though these are outstanding, welcoming family outings. Do NOT append "family" or "kids" to these queries — search the event/culture directly. Run for the Twin Cities AND each outstate metro (Duluth, Rochester, St. Cloud, Mankato, Moorhead, Brainerd, Bemidji, Winona, Alexandria), plus statewide catch-alls:
- "Minnesota cultural festival [MONTH_YEAR]" / "[metro] heritage festival / international festival 2026" / "[metro] multicultural celebration 2026"
- "Minnesota powwow schedule 2026" / "[metro] wacipi 2026" (e.g. Mahkato Wacipi in Mankato, Shakopee Mdewakanton, and reservation-hosted wacipis up north — capture the dates, host nation, and whether the public is welcome, which is nearly always yes)
- "Festival of Nations St Paul 2026" / "[metro] Cinco de Mayo West Side District del Sol 2026" / "[metro] Día de los Muertos 2026"
- "Hmong Freedom Festival J4 St Paul 2026" / "Dragon Festival Lake Phalen 2026" / "Little Mekong Night Market St Paul 2026" / "[metro] Hmong New Year 2026"
- "[metro] Juneteenth 2026" / "Rondo Days St Paul 2026" / "[metro] Somali Independence Day / Somali Week festival 2026"
- "[metro] Diwali festival 2026" / "[metro] Lunar New Year / Tết celebration 2026" / "Irish Fair of Minnesota 2026"
- "[metro] Scandinavian / Nordic / German heritage festival Oktoberfest Svenskarnas Dag 2026" and other community heritage days in the date window
Aggregators to mine each run (`WebFetch` only): **Explore Minnesota** (exploreminnesota.com — cultural/heritage event listings), the metro **CVB cultural-events pages** (Visit Saint Paul, Meet Minneapolis), and each city's **cultural nonprofit calendars** (e.g. CLUES, Hmong American Partnership, the tribal nations' event pages). Fold all into the `events` category; tag with `multicultural`, `cultural-festival`, `heritage`, plus the specific community tag (`hmong`, `somali`, `latino`, `indigenous`, `native-american`, `nordic`, `african-american`, `asian`, etc.) and the city. For Native events/venues, also add the specific nation tag where known — the two primary Minnesota nations are `dakota` (e.g. Shakopee Mdewakanton, Prairie Island, Lower Sioux) and `ojibwe`/`anishinaabe` (e.g. Mille Lacs, Leech Lake, White Earth, Red Lake) — used ALONGSIDE `indigenous`/`native-american`, not instead of them. Use `powwow` as its OWN standalone tag for powwows/wacipis (in addition to `indigenous`/`native-american`) — never treat `powwow` as a substitute for or synonym of the `indigenous` tag, and do not apply `powwow` to non-powwow Indigenous events. Price via `price_type`/`price` (many are free; some charge admission).

### Family-friendly establishments & early-childhood venues (statewide — search the PLACE, not a date):
These are standing venues whose value is open play / recurring programming rather than a one-time event — the family/kids event searches miss them entirely (Rebe's Play Cafe in St. Paul is the canonical example). Search for the venue TYPE, expanding [metro] per the placeholder rule in STEP 2 (core cities + outstate metros + the West/East/North/South suburban quadrant anchors — many of these little play spaces are OUT in the ring suburbs), and give special attention to the babies/toddlers/preschool and teen bands that the rest of the list under-serves:
- "[metro] play cafe / indoor play cafe toddler open play 2026" (e.g. Rebe's Play Cafe, MiniSota — cafés with a play space)
- "[metro] coffee shop with kids play area / play corner / kid-friendly cafe 2026" (many neighborhood cafés keep a toy nook or play corner but never market themselves as a "play café" — these are the easiest to miss and exactly the little places to capture)
- "[metro] bookstore / toy store / library cafe with play area for kids 2026" and "[metro] bakery or ice cream shop with play space for toddlers 2026"
- "[metro] kids hair salon / children's boutique with play area 2026"
- "[metro] indoor playground open play 2026" and "[metro] toddler tot time open gym 2026"
- "[metro] kids gym Little Gym / Romp n Roll baby toddler classes 2026"
- "[metro] baby story time / toddler story time library bookstore 2026"
- "[metro] music class baby toddler (Music Together / Kindermusik) 2026"
- "[metro] trampoline park toddler time 2026" / "[metro] children's museum play 2026"
- "[metro] teen night / teen hangout / teen program 2026" (the older band that "kids" misses)
- "Minnesota ECFE Early Childhood Family Education classes [metro] 2026" (district-run 0–5 programming — a reliable, structured source in every district)
**Discovery techniques (go beyond keyword lists):** small venues rarely self-describe as "play cafés," so mine signals rather than titles — (a) browse Google Maps / Yelp by category near each metro ("cafes", "coffee & tea", "children's amusement centers") and scan listing attributes and photos for a play area; (b) mine reviews and listing text for phrases like "play area", "play corner", "toys for the kids", "great for toddlers", "let the kids play" — a venue with those in its reviews belongs in the guide even if its name is generic; (c) check hyperlocal parent Facebook groups and Macaroni KID city pages, where parents crowd-source exactly these spots; (d) pull neighborhood business directories and "best kid-friendly coffee shops in [metro]" roundups. When a source confirms a play area, add the venue — and if it's one worth guaranteeing every run, promote it into the must-include seed list above.

**Routing (important):** capture the venue itself in the category it best fits — a play café or cafe-with-play-area goes in `restaurants` (tags `family-dining`, `play-area`, plus the age tags like `toddlers`/`babies`); a pure indoor playground/kids' gym with no real food goes in `events` as a recurring open-play entry. Then also capture the venue's scheduled programming (open-play sessions, story times, classes) as `events`, noting the weekly recurrence and any drop-in fee in `description`. Tag with the establishment tags below (`play-cafe`, `indoor-playground`, `tot-time`, `story-time`, `open-play`, `ecfe`) and the right age band.

### Must-include local venues (priority seed list — verify and include EVERY run):
Broad keyword/aggregator searches are non-deterministic: they surface *some* play cafés on a given run but silently miss specific small venues even when those venues are perfectly discoverable (Rebe's Play Cafe slipped through despite being listed in Minnesota Parent and Thrifty Minnesota). This is a hand-maintained allowlist of named local places that MUST appear in every run — treat each as a guaranteed inclusion, not a search suggestion. For each entry: (1) do a quick freshness check (website/Facebook/Google) to confirm it's still open — if it is confirmed permanently closed, log a `dropped_closed_venues` row in STEP 7 and skip it, otherwise include it; (2) fetch a current `website` and a real `image_url` (the normal STEP 4.5→4.8 image pipeline applies, and a `curated_images.csv` row will override if present); (3) emit it in the routed category with the tags shown. These win over search non-determinism — emit the seed rows FIRST (marked `is_seed`), and if a broad search or Facebook result later rediscovers the same place, the **STEP 4.3 precedence & de-duplication pass** keeps this curated seed row and drops the scraped duplicate (salvaging only a real image/deal/website from it). Append new venues here whenever the user names one.

These are hand-verified play cafés / coffee shops with kid play spaces. Because this niche is a specific user priority, re-confirm each is still open every run (rising rents have already closed one — MiniSota Play Cafe, Maple Grove, permanently closed 2026-04-29; do NOT re-add it, and if any below is later confirmed closed, log `dropped_closed_venues` and skip). Route each to `restaurants` unless noted, with `family-dining` + `play-cafe`/`play-area` + `coffee` + the appropriate age tags (`babies`/`toddlers`/`preschool`).

- **Rebe's Play Cafe** — St. Paul (2054 St Clair Ave). Coffee shop + indoor play space for babies/toddlers/preschoolers, open daily 8am–6pm, no entry fee. Route to `restaurants`; tags `family-dining`, `play-cafe`, `play-area`, `coffee`, `babies`, `toddlers`, `preschool`. Website: https://www.rebesplaycafe.com/
- **Sovereign Grounds** — Minneapolis (813 E 48th St). Coffeehouse with a dedicated indoor play room for toddlers/young kids. Tags `family-dining`, `play-cafe`, `play-area`, `coffee`, `toddlers`, `preschool`.
- **Ninth Street Soccer & Coffee** — Minneapolis (801 SE 9th St, 55414). Coffee shop with a kids open-play (soccer) area; open-play hours vary. Tags `family-dining`, `play-cafe`, `play-area`, `coffee`, `toddlers`, `preschool`, `kids`.
- **Ginkgo Coffeehouse** — St. Paul (721 Snelling Ave N, 55104). Long-running neighborhood coffeehouse with a kids' play corner. Tags `family-dining`, `play-cafe`, `play-area`, `coffee`, `toddlers`, `preschool`.
- **Community Grounds** — Columbia Heights (560 40th Ave NE). Café with a kids play area; limited hours (Wed–Sat mornings). Tags `family-dining`, `play-cafe`, `play-area`, `coffee`, `babies`, `toddlers`, `preschool`.
- **Ember and Bean** — Oakdale (7600 2nd St N, Ste B, 55128). Café with a children's play area. Tags `family-dining`, `play-cafe`, `play-area`, `coffee`, `toddlers`, `preschool`.
- **Silverwood Park Café** — St. Anthony (2500 County Rd E, 55421). Park café with family/play space; open daily. Tags `family-dining`, `play-area`, `coffee`, `toddlers`, `preschool`, `family`.
- **Owl's Nest Cafe** — Jordan (231 Broadway St S). Small-town café with a kids play area (southwest metro). Tags `family-dining`, `play-cafe`, `play-area`, `coffee`, `toddlers`, `preschool`.
- **Carma Coffee** — Minneapolis (520 Lowry Ave NE). Car-themed, kid-welcoming coffee shop with a casual kids' corner (not a full play room). Tags `family-dining`, `play-area`, `coffee`, `kids`. Website: https://www.carmacoffeeshop.com/
- **Agra Culture Kitchen at Mia** — Minneapolis (2400 3rd Ave S, inside the Minneapolis Institute of Art). Café with a kids' menu; the play draw is Mia's adjacent Family Center / Family Room, where you can bring your coffee (museum admission is free). Tags `family-dining`, `play-area`, `coffee`, `kids`, `family`. Website: https://www.agra-culture.com/location/minneapolis-institute-of-art-mia/
- **Littles and Lattes Play Cafe** — Baxter (15670 Edgewood Dr, Suite 120) — Brainerd Lakes area. Play café with pretend storefronts, train table, and climbers; open Mon–Sat 9am–3pm. Tags `family-dining`, `play-cafe`, `play-area`, `coffee`, `toddlers`, `preschool`, `brainerd`.
- **Nurture to Play** — Sartell (1001 Second St S, Suite 200) — St. Cloud area. Play café for young kids. Tags `family-dining`, `play-cafe`, `play-area`, `coffee`, `babies`, `toddlers`, `preschool`, `st-cloud`.
- **Peak Café (Edinborough Park)** — Edina (7700 York Ave S). Coffee shop inside Edinborough Park's Adventure Peak indoor play park — parents drink while kids play; café is no-admission, Adventure Peak charges ~$10/child. ### Baked-in Coordinates table (must-include restaurants):
Set `latitude`/`longitude` on each seed restaurant from the values below (match by venue name + location). These were geocoded from each venue's verified street address (OpenStreetMap/Nominatim) and validated inside Minnesota's bounding box (lat 43.3–49.5, lon −97.5 to −89.4). ### Must-include multicultural kid-friendly restaurants & global food halls (priority seed list — verify open and include EVERY run):
Kid-friendly places serving cuisines beyond standard American — so families can introduce kids to global food in a welcoming, casual setting. This list spans global food halls/markets (where each kid can pick a different cuisine — great for picky eaters), East & Southeast Asian, South Asian & Himalayan, African & Middle Eastern, Latin American, and European spots, focused on the Twin Cities metro. Treat each as a guaranteed inclusion: (1) quick freshness check (site/Facebook/Google) to confirm it's still open — this scene turns over, so if any below is later confirmed closed, log a `dropped_closed_venues` row in STEP 7 and skip it; several were recently confirmed CLOSED and are deliberately NOT listed (Keg and Case Market, Fasika Ethiopian, Safari Restaurant, Harry Singh's Caribbean); (2) fetch a current `website` and real `image_url` (normal STEP 4.5→4.8 pipeline); (3) route to `restaurants` with `family-dining` + `kids`/`family` + `multicultural` + the cuisine tags shown; confirm hours each run (several close one weekday — noted below); (4) set `latitude`/`longitude` from the inline **Coordinates:** on each entry (geocoded from the verified street address via OpenStreetMap/Nominatim, validated inside Minnesota's bounding box). Append new spots here whenever the user names one.

*Global food halls & markets (each kid picks a different cuisine):*
- **Midtown Global Market** — Minneapolis (920 E Lake St, 55407). Indoor international market with dozens of family-owned stalls (Mexican, East African, Middle Eastern, Asian, and more) plus a central seating area — a low-pressure way for kids to sample global food. Route to `restaurants`; tags `family-dining`, `food-hall`, `multicultural`, `international`, `kids`, `family`, `minneapolis`, `twin-cities`. Website: https://midtownglobalmarket.org/ — Coordinates: 44.94876, -93.26075
- **The Market at Malcolm Yards** — Minneapolis (501 30th Ave SE, 55414). Modern food hall in a restored Prospect Park industrial building with a range of vendors (tacos, Indian, burgers, dumplings, more) and a self-pour tap wall; roomy family seating. Route to `restaurants`; tags `family-dining`, `food-hall`, `multicultural`, `international`, `kids`, `family`, `minneapolis`, `twin-cities`. Website: https://www.malcolmyardsmarket.com/ — Coordinates: 44.97285, -93.21356
- **Hmong Village** — St. Paul (1001 Johnson Pkwy, 55106). Bustling indoor Hmong marketplace with a large food court serving authentic Hmong and Southeast Asian dishes (papaya salad, egg rolls, noodle soups) — an immersive cultural food outing for families. Route to `restaurants`; tags `family-dining`, `food-hall`, `hmong`, `southeast-asian`, `multicultural`, `kids`, `family`, `st-paul`, `twin-cities`. Coordinates: 44.97180, -93.04537
- **Hmongtown Marketplace** — St. Paul (217 Como Ave, 55103). Indoor/outdoor Hmong market with a popular food court of Hmong and Thai/Lao vendors — casual, affordable, and a great intro to Southeast Asian food for kids. Confirm current hours each run (has run on temporary/limited hours). Route to `restaurants`; tags `family-dining`, `food-hall`, `hmong`, `southeast-asian`, `multicultural`, `kids`, `family`, `st-paul`, `twin-cities`. Coordinates: 44.96126, -93.10956
- **El Burrito Mercado** — St. Paul (175 Cesar Chavez St, 55107). Family-run Mexican market on the West Side with a full taqueria/cantina and hot-food counter — approachable, authentic Mexican in a lively market setting kids enjoy exploring. Route to `restaurants`; tags `family-dining`, `mexican`, `latin-american`, `market`, `multicultural`, `kids`, `family`, `st-paul`, `twin-cities`. Website: https://elburritomercado.com/ — Coordinates: 44.93036, -93.07885 — **RECURRING DEAL (Kids Eat Free — Wednesdays & Sundays after 2pm):** two kids' meals per adult entrée, ages 10 and under. Re-confirm current terms/days against the venue's specials page each run — **the page to use is now http://www.elburritostp.com/specials-events/** (re-checked 2026-10-01: it still publishes "Kids Eat Free Wednesday and Sunday after 2pm (two kids per entrée purchase 10 years and younger)"). The URL previously cited here, `https://elburritomercado.com/restaurant_specials/`, no longer carries any deal text — do not treat that page's silence as the deal having ended. On any run whose date falls on a **Wednesday or Sunday**, set `has_deal: true` with `deal_description`: "Kids eat free Wed & Sun after 2pm — up to 2 kids' meals (age 10 & under) per adult entrée" (leave `deal_expiry` blank since it's ongoing), add tag `kids-eat-free`, and mirror a `meal_deals` row carrying the same terms. On non-Wed/Sun runs, keep El Burrito Mercado as a guaranteed restaurant but drop the deal fields (`has_deal: false`, no mirrored `meal_deals` row).

*East & Southeast Asian:*
- **Quang Restaurant** — Minneapolis (2719 Nicollet Ave S, 55408). Beloved family-run Vietnamese spot on "Eat Street" famous for pho and spring rolls — big, shareable bowls kids like and quick, casual service. **Closed Tuesdays** (note in `description`). Route to `restaurants`; tags `family-dining`, `vietnamese`, `pho`, `southeast-asian`, `multicultural`, `kids`, `family`, `minneapolis`, `twin-cities`. Website: https://www.quangrestaurant.com/ — Coordinates: 44.89020, -93.27812
- **Mandarin Kitchen** — Bloomington (8766 Lyndale Ave S, 55420). Longtime Chinese restaurant known for **weekend dim sum** (cart service) — the little plates make it a fun, interactive meal for kids. **Closed Tuesdays**; dim sum is weekends only (note both in `description`). Route to `restaurants`; tags `family-dining`, `chinese`, `dim-sum`, `east-asian`, `multicultural`, `kids`, `family`, `bloomington`, `twin-cities`. Website: https://www.mandarinkitchenmn.com/ — Coordinates: 44.84447, -93.28936

*South Asian & Himalayan:*
- **Gorkha Palace** — Minneapolis (23 4th St NE, 55413). Family-owned Nepali/Indian/Tibetan restaurant in Northeast with momos (dumplings), mild curries, and naan — approachable spice levels and dumplings kids love. Route to `restaurants`; tags `family-dining`, `nepali`, `indian`, `tibetan`, `himalayan`, `multicultural`, `kids`, `family`, `minneapolis`, `twin-cities`. Website: https://www.gorkhapalace.com/ — Coordinates: 44.98935, -93.25502
- **Everest on Grand** — St. Paul (1278 Grand Ave, 55105). Himalayan restaurant on Grand Avenue serving Nepali, Indian, and Tibetan dishes (momos, curries) in a warm, family-welcoming room. Route to `restaurants`; tags `family-dining`, `nepali`, `himalayan`, `indian`, `tibetan`, `multicultural`, `kids`, `family`, `st-paul`, `twin-cities`. Website: https://www.everestongrand.com/ — Coordinates: 44.93991, -93.15412

*African & Middle Eastern:*
- **Holy Land** — Minneapolis (2513 Central Ave NE, 55418). Iconic Middle Eastern deli, bakery, and grocery in Northeast with a hot-food counter (shawarma, falafel, hummus, fresh pita) — casual counter service and a market to explore. Route to `restaurants`; tags `family-dining`, `middle-eastern`, `mediterranean`, `deli`, `market`, `multicultural`, `kids`, `family`, `minneapolis`, `twin-cities`. Website: https://www.holylandbrand.com/ — Coordinates: 45.03574, -93.24719
- **Bolé Ethiopian Cuisine** — St. Paul (1341 Pascal St N, 55108). Family-run Ethiopian restaurant where you eat with injera bread instead of utensils — the hands-on, shareable platters make it a fun, interactive meal for kids. **Closed Mondays** (note in `description`). Route to `restaurants`; tags `family-dining`, `ethiopian`, `east-african`, `multicultural`, `kids`, `family`, `st-paul`, `twin-cities`. Website: https://www.boleethiopian.com/ — Coordinates: 44.98172, -93.16209
- **Mama Safia's Kitchen** — Minneapolis (720 E Lake St, 55407). Family-run Somali/East African restaurant near Midtown serving sambusas, rice dishes, and mild, kid-approachable flavors in a welcoming setting. Route to `restaurants`; tags `family-dining`, `somali`, `east-african`, `multicultural`, `kids`, `family`, `minneapolis`, `twin-cities`. Coordinates: 44.94865, -93.26360

*Latin American:*
- **Andale Taqueria y Mercado** — Richfield (7700 Nicollet Ave S, 55423). Authentic Mexican taqueria attached to a Latin market — tacos, tortas, and aguas frescas at a counter, with a grocery to browse; casual and very kid-friendly. Route to `restaurants`; tags `family-dining`, `mexican`, `latin-american`, `taqueria`, `market`, `multicultural`, `kids`, `family`, `richfield`, `twin-cities`. Coordinates: 44.86343, -93.27885

*European:*
- **Kramarczuk's East European Deli** — Minneapolis (215 E Hennepin Ave, 55414). Historic Northeast Ukrainian/Eastern European deli (since 1954) with sausages, pierogi, and cabbage rolls served cafeteria-style — walk the line, point, and go; easy with kids. Route to `restaurants`; tags `family-dining`, `ukrainian`, `eastern-european`, `deli`, `multicultural`, `kids`, `family`, `minneapolis`, `twin-cities`. Website: https://www.kramarczuks.com/ — Coordinates: 44.98784, -93.25727
- **Black Forest Inn** — Minneapolis (1 E 26th St, 55404). Longtime German restaurant (since 1965) with schnitzel, spaetzle, and a leafy beer garden; a relaxed, kid-welcoming daytime/dinner spot (confirm current family hours each run). Route to `restaurants`; tags `family-dining`, `german`, `european`, `multicultural`, `kids`, `family`, `minneapolis`, `twin-cities`. Website: https://www.blackforestinnmpls.com/ — Coordinates: 44.95538, -93.27762
- **FIKA at the American Swedish Institute** — Minneapolis (2600 Park Ave, 55407). Nordic café inside the American Swedish Institute (a castle-like museum) serving Scandinavian small plates and pastries — pair a light, kid-friendly meal with a museum visit. Confirm café hours each run (tied to museum hours). Route to `restaurants`; tags `family-dining`, `nordic`, `scandinavian`, `swedish`, `cafe`, `multicultural`, `kids`, `family`, `minneapolis`, `twin-cities`. ### Must-include standing attractions & water destinations (priority seed list — include EVERY run):
These are permanent, well-known family destinations that are stable year to year — the Minnesota Zoo, indoor play parks, seasonal aquatic parks, swimming beaches, and splash pads. Because they don't change, there's **no need to rediscover them by scraping each day**: emit each as a guaranteed seed row (marked `is_seed`) with its baked-in coordinates below, so it always maps with a pin. **What still must be checked each run is season + hours, not existence:** many of these are summer-only (Memorial Day → Labor Day) or have seasonal hours, so on each run do a quick freshness check of the venue's own page for current open dates/hours and put them in `description` — and if a seasonal spot is currently closed for the season, still emit it but say so in `description` (e.g. "outdoor beach — swimming season typically Memorial Day–Labor Day; confirm current hours"). If any is permanently closed, log a `dropped_closed_venues` row in STEP 7 and skip it. The normal STEP 4.5→4.8 image pipeline (and any `curated_images.csv` override) applies. Append new standing destinations here whenever the user names one.

*Attractions & indoor play (route to `events` as a standing/recurring attraction; `price_type` per the venue — the Zoo and Edinborough charge admission):*
- **Minnesota Zoo** — Apple Valley (13000 Zoo Blvd, 55124). Year-round zoo with indoor tropical trail, outdoor Northern Trail, farm, and the Treetop Trail elevated walk — a marquee all-ages, all-weather destination. Route to `events`; `price_type: Paid`; tags `attraction`, `zoo`, `animals`, `indoor`, `outdoor`, `kids`, `family`, `apple-valley`, `south-metro`, `twin-cities`. Coordinates: 44.77004, -93.19572. Website: https://mnzoo.org/
- **Edinborough Park** — Edina (7700 York Ave S, 55435). Indoor park with Adventure Peak (a large climbing/play structure), an indoor pool, track, and amphitheater — a reliable rainy-day/winter destination. Route to `events`; `price_type: Paid` (Adventure Peak charges per child; note current fee/hours in `description`); tags `attraction`, `indoor-playground`, `play-area`, `pool`, `kids`, `family`, `edina`, `west-metro`, `twin-cities`. Coordinates: 44.86276, -93.32197.

*Cultural & heritage institutions (route to `events` as a standing attraction; these are permanent museums/cultural centers — confirm current hours + any special exhibit each run, and note in `description` when they participate in the Museums for All EBT/SNAP or MELSA smARTpass discount programs from the Deals & discounts section, so the deal surfaces too):*
- **American Swedish Institute** — Minneapolis (2600 Park Ave, 55407). Turreted mansion + modern wing with Nordic art, hands-on family programming, and FIKA café; a marquee heritage destination. Museums for All participant. Route to `events`; `price_type: Paid`; tags `attraction`, `museum`, `cultural`, `heritage`, `nordic`, `kids`, `family`, `minneapolis`, `twin-cities`. Coordinates: 44.95500, -93.26583. Website: https://asimn.org/
- **The Museum of Russian Art (TMORA)** — Minneapolis (5500 Stevens Ave S, 55419). The only North American museum devoted to Russian art and culture, in a former church near Diamond Lake Rd/35W. Route to `events`; `price_type: Paid`; tags `attraction`, `museum`, `cultural`, `heritage`, `russian`, `kids`, `family`, `minneapolis`, `twin-cities`. Coordinates: 44.90333, -93.27583. Website: https://tmora.org/
- **Somali Museum of Minnesota** — Minneapolis (2925 Chicago Ave, 55407, in the Midtown Exchange building; open Thu–Sun — confirm current hours each run). North America's only museum of Somali culture, with 1,000+ nomadic artifacts and cross-cultural programming. Route to `events`; `price_type` per current policy; tags `attraction`, `museum`, `cultural`, `heritage`, `somali`, `east-african`, `kids`, `family`, `minneapolis`, `twin-cities`. Coordinates: 44.94876, -93.26075. Website: https://somalimuseum.org/
- **Hmong Cultural Center Museum** — St. Paul (375 University Ave W, Ste 204, 55103). Small museum on the Hmong experience, textiles, and instruments. Geocode the address via the resolver; confirm hours each run. Route to `events`; tags `attraction`, `museum`, `cultural`, `heritage`, `hmong`, `southeast-asian`, `kids`, `family`, `st-paul`, `twin-cities`. Website: https://hmongculturalcentermuseum.org/
- **Mille Lacs Indian Museum & Trading Post** — Onamia (43411 Oodena Dr, 56359). Minnesota Historical Society site on Ojibwe/Anishinaabe history and living culture; trading post admission is free. Greater-MN destination (~2 hr north of the metro). Geocode via the resolver; confirm season/hours each run. Route to `events`; tags `attraction`, `museum`, `cultural`, `heritage`, `indigenous`, `native-american`, `ojibwe`, `kids`, `family`, `onamia`, `greater-mn`. Website: https://www.mnhs.org/millelacs
- **Hjemkomst Center** — Moorhead (202 1st Ave N, 56560; phone 218-299-5511). City-run heritage museum on the Red River housing a full-scale replica Viking ship (the *Hjemkomst*, sailed to Norway in 1982) and a hand-built Norwegian stave church replica, plus rotating exhibits from the Historical & Cultural Society of Clay County — the marquee family/history destination in the Fargo-Moorhead area. Route to `events` as a standing attraction (blank date); `price_type: Paid` (public admission; confirm current rates + hours each run). Tags `attraction`, `museum`, `cultural`, `heritage`, `nordic`, `norwegian`, `viking`, `history`, `indoor`, `kids`, `family`, `all-ages`, `moorhead`, `clay-county`, `greater-mn`. Website: https://www.moorheadmn.gov/parks-rec/hjemkomst-center/ Coordinates: 46.8780360, -96.7779860.

Route to `events`; tag `cultural`/`heritage` + the specific community + city.

Museums and performing-arts venues that families can reach cheaply or free — most are **MELSA smARTpass** partners, meaning a Twin Cities library card gets free or discounted admission for up to two people. Emit each as a guaranteed seed row (marked `is_seed`) with its baked coordinates; the standing-deal overlay (STEP 4.35) attaches the smARTpass terms. **Route each to `events` as a standing/recurring attraction** (same treatment as the marquee attractions below) — confirm current hours and any seasonal closure each run. Normal image pipeline applies.

- **Walker Art Center** — Minneapolis (725 Vineland Pl, 55403). Contemporary art museum attached to the **free** Minneapolis Sculpture Garden (Spoonbridge and Cherry) — free gallery days, family art-making programs and a well-regarded kids' film series. `price_type: Paid` (sculpture garden always free; gallery free on select days — confirm each run). ### Must-include community centers & public indoor pools (priority seed list — verify open + current public schedule EVERY run):
Municipal recreation/community centers and public aquatic facilities that offer **indoor pools and/or indoor playgrounds with open-to-the-public swim or play times** (free or paid, no membership required) — reliable all-weather, all-ages outings the broad searches miss. Emit each as a guaranteed seed row with baked coordinates; route to `events` as a standing/recurring attraction. `price_type: Paid` for most (drop-in day pass), but note the **free/low-cost** ones and any free-swim windows in `description`. What must be checked each run is the **current open-swim / open-play schedule**, not existence — several are strongly seasonal (the indoor waterparks at community centers often CLOSE for summer when the city's outdoor pools open, then reopen in fall), and toddler/open-swim windows change, so confirm live hours from the facility's page and put them in `description`. For YMCA-operated municipal centers, note that public **drop-in day passes** are available (not members-only). Normal STEP 4.5→4.8 image pipeline applies. Tags `attraction`, `community-center`, `indoor`, `active-play`, plus `pool`/`aquatics` and/or `indoor-playground`, `kids`, `family` + the metro tag. Append new centers/pools here whenever the user names one.

### Must-include neighborhood parks (priority seed list — verify and include EVERY run):
Small city/neighborhood parks and destination playgrounds that the broad park searches miss (they surface state/regional/destination parks far more reliably than a specific local playground). These carry forward in `parks.csv` like the rest of the evergreen park list, but are ALSO re-asserted here so a named park the user asked for is never dropped by CSV churn.

> **Suburban neighborhood-park sweep (baked into `parks.csv`, added July 29, 2026):** the evergreen `parks.csv` now includes ~510 smaller suburban neighborhood parks with playgrounds across 25 metro cities (West: Minnetonka, Plymouth, Eden Prairie, Maple Grove, Wayzata, Hopkins; East: Woodbury, Stillwater, Cottage Grove, Maplewood, Oakdale, White Bear Lake; North: Blaine, Coon Rapids, Anoka, Andover, Shoreview, Fridley; South: Bloomington, Burnsville, Eagan, Apple Valley, Lakeville, Shakopee, Prior Lake). These were built the same way as the Minneapolis/St. Paul citywide sweep — OSM `leisure=playground` points spatially matched to named `leisure=park` polygons — carrying `title`=park name, `address`="City, MN" (city-only, not street), coords, `image_source: curated_category`, and tags `playground[,splash-pad],neighborhood-park,<city-slug>,<direction>-metro,twin-cities,free,outdoor`. **Because many suburb names repeat in other states (Bloomington IN, Plymouth MA, Apple Valley CA, etc.), every swept row was filtered to the Twin Cities metro bounding box (lat 44.4–45.5, lon −94.2 to −92.5); any future re-sweep MUST apply the same bbox filter to avoid pulling in out-of-state parks.** They live in `parks.csv` and carry forward automatically; the named destination playgrounds below remain listed individually because they're specific venues the user asked for by name. For each: confirm it still exists (city parks page / Google), route to `parks` (`price_type: Free`), fill `address`/`website`/`tags`, and let the normal image pipeline assign a photo. Coverage now spans all four metro directions (West / North / South / East) to mirror the STEP 2 suburban expansion — regional *reserves* (Elm Creek, French, Lebanon Hills, Bunker Hills, Lake Elmo) are deliberately NOT listed here because the broad park searches already surface them reliably; this list is for the smaller city-park destination playgrounds they miss. Append new neighborhood parks here whenever the user names one.

**Core cities (Minneapolis / St. Paul):**
- **Mattocks Park** — St. Paul (441 Macalester St, 55105), Macalester-Groveland neighborhood. Playground and tot-lot, two tennis courts, half-court basketball, outdoor pickleball courts, and open fields for softball/soccer/football/ultimate; picnic areas, benches, drinking fountains; open sunrise–11pm. Tags `playground`, `tot-lot`, `tennis`, `pickleball`, `basketball`, `sports-fields`, `picnic`, `st-paul`, `twin-cities`. Website: https://www.stpaul.gov/facilities/mattocks-park

**West metro:**
- **Central Park** — Maple Grove (12000 Central Park Way, 55369). Massive multi-zone destination playground ("like five parks in one"): seven play zones with rubberized accessible surfacing, a ~24-ft climbing tower, four giant slides, a ~120-ft climbing wall, unique swings, plus a splash pad, amphitheater, and walking paths around the pond. Tags `playground`, `destination-playground`, `splash-pad`, `accessible`, `maple-grove`, `west-metro`, `twin-cities`. Website: https://www.maplegrovemn.gov/505/Central-Park
- **Round Lake Park** — Eden Prairie (16691 Valley View Rd, 55346). City park with a marine-themed splash/water-play area, a treehouse-style play structure and a merry-go-round, plus a sandy swimming beach, picnic space, and lake trails (kayak/paddleboard rental in season). Tags `playground`, `splash-pad`, `beach`, `picnic`, `eden-prairie`, `west-metro`, `twin-cities`. Website: https://www.edenprairiemn.gov/ (confirm the Round Lake Park page each run)
- **Big Willow Park** — Minnetonka (11522 Minnetonka Blvd, 55305). Community preserve and athletic complex with a large accessible play area, athletic fields, and paved trails through the preserve. Tags `playground`, `accessible`, `sports-fields`, `trails`, `minnetonka`, `west-metro`, `twin-cities`. Website: https://www.minnetonkamn.gov/ (confirm the Big Willow page each run)

**North metro:**
- **Aquatore Park** — Blaine (9191 Lincoln St NE, 55434). Popular city park with a large playground and tot-lot, paved trails, picnic areas with grills, and a fishing pier. Tags `playground`, `tot-lot`, `picnic`, `trails`, `blaine`, `north-metro`, `twin-cities`. Website: https://www.blainemn.gov/facilities/facility/details/aquatore-park-29

- **Woodhaven Park (Destination All-Play)** — Eagan (1950 Gold Trail, 55122). Eagan's inclusive Destination All-Play playground built for all abilities: rubberized accessible trails, bankshot basketball, swings and slides of every size, plus picnic space and open field. ### STEP 2 — MANDATORY direct iCal feed pull (run on EVERY build — these feeds are INVISIBLE to web search):
**Why this is its own hard step, not a bullet in the guidance below:** the sources here publish raw iCalendar (`.ics`) feeds. Search engines never index `.ics` files, so the STEP 2 query batches will NEVER surface them — this is the exact reason hyperlocal library storytimes and nature-center programs were missing from the feed. Merely listing these URLs under "structured feeds" did NOT get them fetched. You MUST explicitly `WebFetch` each URL below every run, parse the VEVENTs, and fold the in-window ones into `events`. This step is not optional and not search-gated. Do it after the search batches, before STEP 3.

**Feeds to pull — fetch each one, every run (do NOT substitute a web search, do NOT skip):**
- Dodge Nature Center — `https://www.dodgenaturecenter.org/events/?ical=1`
- Washington County Library — `https://washcolib.libcal.com/ical_subscribe.php?src=p&cid=10166`
- Dakota County Library — `https://dakotacountylibrary.libcal.com/ical_subscribe.php?src=p&cid=9306`
- Anoka County Library — `https://anokacounty.libcal.com/ical_subscribe.php?src=p&cid=9233` (⚠️ ~730KB — exceeds WebFetch's ~450KB fetch limit, so it truncates in July and normally yields 0 in-window; fetch anyway but expect to log it truncated — see the size caveat in the parse steps below)
- Scott County Library — `https://scottlib.libcal.com/ical_subscribe.php?src=p&cid=10242`
- Carver County Library — `https://carverlib.libcal.com/ical_subscribe.php?src=p&cid=9314`
- Great River Regional Library — `https://events.griver.org/ical_subscribe.php?src=p&cid=18620` (LOW-YIELD: cid=18620 is the region-wide admin/board-meeting calendar; keep the fetch for completeness but expect little family programming. For St. Cloud family programming, also try the branch cids in `event-source-calendars.md` — best-effort, confirm each run)
- Visit Bemidji (CVB, greater-MN) — `https://www.visitbemidji.com/events-calendar/?ical=1` (✅ verified Aug 17, 2026 — small clean ~18KB `.ics`, real Bemidji-area family/community events: preschool nature programs, farmers markets, concerts, BSU games. This is a broad CVB calendar, not a library feed, but it's a machine-readable `.ics` that keyword search never indexes, so it belongs in the mandatory pull. Tag results `bemidji`.)
- East Central Regional Library (ECRL) — `https://ecrlib.libcal.com/ical_subscribe.php?src=p&cid=13690` (✅ verified Aug 21, 2026 — LibCal, same feed shape and parse rules as the county LibCal feeds above; covers east-central MN: Cambridge, Isanti, North Branch, Chisago Lakes/Chisago City, Milaca, Mille Lacs/Isle, Mora, Pine City, Princeton, Rush City, Hinckley, Sandstone. Same past-front-loaded, ~500-event cap — use the oriented "read-to-the-end" prompt. LOCATION carries the **branch name** (e.g. "North Branch Area Library", "Chisago Lakes Area Library"), not a street address, so derive the city tag from the branch name — the branch name contains its city.)
- Kitchigami Regional Library (KRLS) — `https://krls.org/feed/my-calendar-ics/` (✅ verified Aug 21, 2026 — WordPress "My Calendar" ICS, north-central MN: Brainerd/Baxter, Pine River, Park Rapids, Walker, Longville, Pequot Lakes, Nisswa, Wadena, Backus, Remer, and other Cass/Crow Wing/Wadena/Todd-area branches. Unlike the LibCal feeds, this one carries the **FULL street address** in LOCATION — e.g. "Park Rapids Area Library 210 W. First St. Park Rapids Minnesota 56470" — so it geocodes directly, no branch→city map needed. Real event URLs are `krls.org/mc-events/<slug>/`. It also front-loads a few past events, so keep only in-window. This supersedes the older "thin / low-yield" note for KRLS in the reliable-feeds list below.)

> **Do NOT add the Family Fun Twin Cities aggregator iCal to this mandatory pull** — this list is primary-source feeds only. (Family Fun may still be used as an OPTIONAL cross-check backstop elsewhere, but it is deliberately excluded here.)

**How to fetch + parse each feed (repeat for every URL above):**
1. `WebFetch` the URL (WebFetch only — never bash/curl/requests, per the web-content policy). **CRITICAL — orient the fetch to the tail, or you WILL get "0 events."** These LibCal feeds are sorted by DTSTART **ascending**, and the first ~1 month of entries are already **past** (the feeds start ~4 weeks before TODAY). A naive "list the in-window events" prompt makes WebFetch read only the past head of the feed and wrongly report zero future events. Instead prompt it EXACTLY like this: *"This iCal (.ics) feed is sorted by DTSTART ascending; the earliest entries are already in the past. Read all the way to the END of the feed — do NOT stop at the early/past entries and do NOT report zero based on them. Report (a) the single latest DTSTART date anywhere in the feed, and (b) every VEVENT whose DTSTART falls on or between `<TODAY>` and `<END_DATE>` — for each give SUMMARY, DTSTART (date + time), LOCATION, URL, and a short DESCRIPTION, prioritizing the nearest-term dates. Scan to the bottom before answering."* (Verification signal: WebFetch should return a **latest DTSTART well after TODAY** — often 3–6 weeks out. If it instead reports a latest date within a few days of the feed's start / only past events, the feed was truncated at fetch time — see the size caveat below — so log it as truncated, not empty.) This oriented prompt is what makes the mid-size feeds (~300–450KB: Washington, Dakota, Scott, Carver) return real upcoming events instead of zero.
   - **Size caveat (Anoka):** WebFetch hard-truncates the fetched content at roughly ~450KB, dropping everything after that point. The **Anoka County Library** feed is ~730KB, so WebFetch never reaches the current window (it truncates back in July) and this feed will keep returning only past/near-empty results no matter how the prompt is worded — this is a fetch-size limit, not a prompt problem, and there is no server-side date/audience/branch filter that shrinks it (all tested and ignored). Still fetch Anoka every run for completeness, but when it returns only past events, log it as `source_unreachable` (reason: feed too large for WebFetch) rather than treating the empty result as surprising. The other five LibCal feeds and Dodge are under the limit and work with the oriented prompt.
2. Keep only VEVENTs with DTSTART in `[TODAY, END_DATE]`. Discard past events and anything after END_DATE — these feeds carry out-of-window items (both prior and future).
3. Drop non-programming/administrative entries: library-closure notices ("Libraries Closed - …"), board/finance/work-session meetings, staff-only items, and anything clearly not a public family/community activity. KEEP storytimes, baby/toddler play, craft/STEM/maker programs, concerts, author visits, dog-reading, teen nights, camps, and naturalist programs.
4. Map each kept VEVENT into the STEP 3 events schema: `title`=SUMMARY; `date`=DTSTART date (YYYY-MM-DD); `time`=DTSTART local time (or "All day" if none); `address`=LOCATION (lat/long fill in the normal geocode pass); `website`=the VEVENT URL if present, else the system's public site; `description`=a trimmed DESCRIPTION (strip HTML; summarizing registration/"sold out" boilerplate is fine); `price_type`=`free` for library programs unless the DESCRIPTION states a fee; set `age_range` + age tags from the audience wording (baby/toddler/preschool/school-age/teen/family); add category tags (`story-time`, `class`, `camp`, `music-class`, `recurring`, etc.) and the correct **city tag** (the actual suburb, never a generic "Twin Cities"). For a recurring series, use the next in-window occurrence's date and note the recurrence in `description`.

   **MANDATORY content tag — every LibCal/BiblioCommons library-feed row MUST carry the `library` tag, in addition to any `src:`/system plumbing tags.** These feeds arrive with only provenance tags (e.g. `feed,scott_library,src:scott_libcal`) and no *content* tags, and a row with no content tag has nothing for the STEP 4.8 curated overlay to match on — so it drops through to the generic `curated_category` events rotation pool and can be stamped with an obviously-wrong image (a bilingual **storytime** card shipped a **fire-truck** photo on 2026-08-28 for exactly this reason). Tagging is what routes these rows to a correct topical image: `curated_images.csv` carries `tag,library` and `tag,story-time` fallback images, and a `tag` match is more specific than the `category` pool, so it wins. Concretely, for every kept library VEVENT: (a) always add `library`; (b) add `story-time` whenever the SUMMARY/DESCRIPTION indicates a read-aloud/song/rhyme program — match `stor`, `song`, `rhyme`, `read`, `books`, `cuentos`, `tiempo de cuentos` (Spanish/bilingual storytimes count — the fire-truck row was titled "Stories and a Song Bilingual (English/Spanish)"); (c) add the other applicable content tags from the list above (`class`, `camp`, `music-class`, etc.). A library row must **never** reach STEP 4.8 carrying only plumbing tags.
5. Fold the results into `events`. Do NOT pre-dedupe against the search results yourself — the unified **STEP 4.3** precedence/de-dup pass collapses any overlap with a search-found copy of the same program.
6. Resilience: if a feed is unreachable, times out, returns non-iCal, or has zero in-window events, log a `pipeline` `info` row (issue_type `source_unreachable`, item = the feed name) and move on — never a hard failure.

**Mandatory contribution log (this is our verification signal — always write it):** at STEP 7, for EACH feed above, log a `pipeline` `info` row (issue_type `ical_feed_pull`, item = feed name) recording how many in-window events it contributed this run (e.g. "Washington County Library iCal: 14 events folded into events"). The expected healthy pattern is that Dodge and the five under-limit LibCal feeds (Washington, Dakota, Scott, Carver — and Great River low-yield) each contribute at least a few events; only Anoka is expected to come back empty/truncated. If **all five under-limit LibCal feeds** return zero on a normal build, that is a red flag (it usually means the oriented "read-to-the-end" prompt regressed to a naive one and WebFetch only read the past head) — additionally log a `warning`-severity `pipeline` row (issue_type `ical_pull_empty`) so we catch it. (Anoka alone returning zero is expected and is NOT a red flag.)

### Reliable structured feeds (prioritize — this is the feed-reliability focus):
When a source offers a structured feed, prefer it over free-text scraping — it yields cleaner dates, deep links, and real photos, and reduces the STEP 4.5/4.6 error-fixer backlog. Fetch all of these with `WebFetch` only (never bash/curl/requests), per the web-content policy:
- Team/league schedule pages (MLB/MiLB, NHL, NBA, MLS, and college sites — many colleges run on the Sidearm Sports platform with a predictable structure and per-game deep links).
- Pages carrying `schema.org/Event` JSON-LD markup (many venue/ticketing pages embed it) — parse the structured event objects (name, startDate, location, offers/price, image, url) directly from the fetched HTML.
