- **Named primary source calendars (fetch each directly EVERY run — this is our own curated source list; the full annotated version with status flags and platform notes lives in `event-source-calendars.md`).** These are the specific municipal calendars where hyperlocal family programming (park storytimes, splash-pad pop-ups, puppet shows, movies in the park, naturalist programs) is published — the events generic keyword searches reliably miss. Fetch each with `WebFetch`, requesting the current date window (TODAY → END_DATE); fold anything family-appropriate into `events` and let STEP 4.3 de-dupe collapse overlaps. Treat any unreachable/empty/blocked source as "log a `pipeline` `info` row (issue_type `source_unreachable`) and carry on" — never a hard failure.
  - *iCal/ICS feeds:* the primary-source iCal feeds — **Dodge Nature Center + the six county-library LibCal feeds** — are pulled by the **MANDATORY direct iCal feed pull sub-step above**; that step is now the single source of truth for their URLs and parsing rules, so don't re-implement them from here. Three Rivers Park District `https://www.threeriversparks.org/programs` has NO public machine-readable feed (registration runs on WEBTRAC/Vermont Systems, which blocks direct fetches) — fetch the HTML `/programs` page as a best-effort source and treat empty/blocked results as a `source_unreachable` info row, not a failure. (The Family Fun Twin Cities Events Manager iCal `https://www.familyfuntwincities.com/family-fun-events-calendar/?ical=1` may be used as an OPTIONAL cross-check backstop only — it is deliberately NOT part of the mandatory pull — filter strictly to the run's date window.)
  - *County library systems (each aggregates every branch's kids/family programs):* Hennepin `https://hclib.bibliocommons.com/v2/events`, Saint Paul `https://sppl.bibliocommons.com/v2/events`, Ramsey `https://rclreads.bibliocommons.com/v2/events` (all BiblioCommons — fetch each directly and filter by audience Baby/Preschool/School Age/Family/Teen). The six suburban **LibCal** systems (Washington, Dakota, Anoka, Scott, Carver, Great River) are handled by the MANDATORY direct iCal feed pull sub-step above — see there for their `ical_subscribe.php` URLs and the Great River low-yield caveat.
  - *Regional park districts, county parks & nature centers:* MPRB `https://www.minneapolisparks.org/event-calendar/`, Saint Paul Parks `https://www.stpaul.gov/parkevents`, Ramsey County Parks `https://ramseycounty.civicrec.com/MN/ramsey-county-parks-recreation/catalog`, Washington County Parks `https://www.washingtoncountymn.gov/532/Programs-and-Events`, Dakota County Parks `https://www.dakotacountymn.gov/parks/programs-events`, Anoka County Parks `https://www.anokacountyparks.com/activities-events`; Tamarack Nature Center `https://www.ramseycountymn.gov/residents/parks-recreation/tamarack-nature-center/programs-events`, Wood Lake Nature Center `https://www.richfieldmn.gov/Calendar.aspx?EID=1387`, Bell Museum `https://www.bellmuseum.umn.edu/events/`.
  - *City Parks & Rec (mid-size suburbs — verified live):* Eagan `https://cityofeagan.com/calendar`, Woodbury `https://www.woodburymn.gov/412/Special-Events`, Maple Grove `https://www.maplegrovemn.gov/calendar.aspx?CID=23`, Edina `https://www.edinamn.gov/calendar.aspx?CID=22`, Richfield `https://anc.apm.activecommunities.com/richfieldrecreation/home` (ActiveNet), Roseville `https://www.cityofroseville.com/calendar.aspx?CID=26`, Cottage Grove `https://www.cottagegrovemn.gov/Calendar.aspx`, Oakdale `https://www.oakdalemn.gov/calendar.aspx?CID=22`. Also attempt (confirm each run): Plymouth, Bloomington, St. Louis Park, Eden Prairie, Burnsville, Apple Valley, Shakopee (see `event-source-calendars.md`).
    - *CivicPlus RSS feed pattern (machine-readable — prefer over scraping the HTML `Calendar.aspx` page):* many CivicPlus cities expose a raw RSS event feed at `https://<cityhost>/RSSFeed.aspx?ModID=58&CID=All-calendar.xml` — each `<item>` is an event with a real `Calendar.aspx?EID=<id>` deep link. ✅ verified Aug 21, 2026 for **Burnsville** `https://www.burnsvillemn.gov/RSSFeed.aspx?ModID=58&CID=All-calendar.xml` (~31 items: open skate, stick-and-puck, rec programs) and **Lakeville** `https://www.lakevillemn.gov/RSSFeed.aspx?ModID=58&CID=All-calendar.xml` (~8 items; some council/meeting noise — filter to family/rec). The event's real date is inside the item description, not `<pubDate>` (pubDate is the publish timestamp), so read the description for the date. This is the same pattern already used for Richfield/Maplewood/South St. Paul; try it for any CivicPlus city before falling back to HTML. ⚠️ Not universal — some CivicPlus cities return HTTP 200 but zero items on ModID=58 (Hopkins, Cottage Grove observed empty) or redirect (Eagan), so confirm item count each run and treat empty as `source_unreachable`, not a failure.
  - *Greater-MN outstate regional sources (Duluth, St. Cloud, Bemidji, Brainerd, Grand Rapids — added Aug 17, 2026; full annotated list in `event-source-calendars.md` §6):* These five outstate metros have far fewer clean feeds than the metro, so coverage leans on the STEP 2 regional search batches + HTML best-effort. Two are machine-readable and should be fetched every run:
    - **DECC events API (Duluth)** — `https://decc.org/wp-json/wp/v2/events?per_page=100` (✅ verified Aug 17, 2026 — WordPress REST JSON, ~86 items with `event_start_date`/`event_end_date` in `YYYYMMDD`; big-venue concerts, festivals, family shows). `WebFetch` it, parse the JSON list, keep items whose start date is in `[TODAY, END_DATE]`, tag `duluth`.
    - **DNR state-park naturalist programs (STATEWIDE JSON — fetch every run)** — `https://webapps.dnr.state.mn.us/calendar/app?jsp=json&o=startdate&o=starttime&k=stpk&p=thismonth&ae=y` (✅ verified Aug 21, 2026 — official MN DNR calendar JSON, ~170+ family-perfect programs statewide: junior park naturalist, guided hikes, bat walks, candlelight events, "I Can…" outdoor-skills series, etc.). This is high value because it spreads across greater-MN state parks that the metro-centric feeds never touch, and keyword search does not index it. `WebFetch` this URL AND the next-month view `…&p=nextmonth…` (swap `p=thismonth`→`p=nextmonth`). Each JSON `events[]` item has `title`, `startdate`/`enddate` (`YYYY-MM-DD`), `starttime`/`endtime`, `location` (the park name, e.g. "Wild River State Park"), `location_url`, `description`, `directions`, and `contact`. Map: `title`←title; `date`←startdate (for a multi-day range, emit the in-window occurrence); `time`←starttime; `address`← the park name (the geocode pass resolves the park's city — or mine the `directions` text for the nearest town); `website`← the item `url` if non-empty else `https://www.dnr.state.mn.us`+`location_url`; `price_type`=`free` (state-park programs are free; a vehicle permit is separate — do not mark as paid); city tag = the park's city. Keep only items with startdate in `[TODAY, END_DATE]`. Tag `state-park`, `nature`, `outdoors`.
    - **Visit Bemidji iCal** — handled by the MANDATORY direct iCal feed pull sub-step above.
    - Best-effort / confirm-each-run (◐ — no clean feed confirmed; `WebFetch` HTML and treat empty/blocked as a `source_unreachable` info row): Grand Rapids Public Library BiblioCommons `https://grpl.bibliocommons.com/v2/events` (audience filters, tag `grand-rapids`); Lake Agassiz Regional Library (LARL, northwest MN — Moorhead, Detroit Lakes, Fergus Falls, Barnesville, Ada, Hawley, Mahnomen) runs on **Communico** (`api.communico.co`) — its public events page is `https://www.larl.org/events`; fetch that HTML page for the date window (no clean public iCal confirmed without an API key), tag by branch city; Great River Regional Library St. Cloud-area branch cids; Duluth Children's Museum, Great Lakes Aquarium, Reif Center (Grand Rapids), Paramount (St. Cloud) HTML calendars; each region's CVB (Visit Duluth, Visit St. Cloud, Explore Brainerd Lakes, Visit Grand Rapids MN) and Community Education portal (Eleyo/RecDesk/CivicRec — registration UIs, HTML-scrape only). (Kitchigami Regional Library is now a ✅ verified feed in the MANDATORY direct iCal pull above — no longer best-effort.)
- (Legacy generic note, still applies to sources beyond the named list above:) other library systems (LibCal / Communico) and Parks & Rec registration systems (ActiveNet / CivicRec) offer consistent per-metro calendars — prefer their structured feeds when found.
- Eventbrite / Ticketmaster venue and organizer pages for ticketed family shows.
- **Broad, non-keyword-filtered community event calendars (important — these catch the events the family/kids searches miss, like car shows and cruise nights):** the Explore Minnesota statewide events calendar (official state tourism feed), each metro's Convention & Visitors Bureau / tourism calendar (e.g. Visit Duluth, Experience Rochester, Visit St. Cloud, Visit Mankato, Visit Bemidji, etc.), city/regional Chamber of Commerce event calendars, and local newspaper / TV-station community calendars. These list EVERYTHING happening in a place regardless of audience tag, so pull the date window from them and fold anything family-appropriate into the relevant category. This is the primary structural fix for missed special-interest events — don't rely only on keyword searches.
- **Hyperlocal family/parenting directories (important — these catch standing venues like play cafés and early-childhood programming):** these are venue-oriented roundups that list the establishments and recurring kid programming the event searches miss — Family Fun Twin Cities, Twin Cities Family, Minnesota Parent (its venue directory + indoor-play lists), Thrifty Minnesota, Explore Minnesota's indoor-playground guide, and Macaroni KID (per-city hyperlocal family-event newsletters — run the city nearest each metro). Mine these for play cafés, indoor playgrounds, kids' gyms, tot times, and story-time series, then route venues vs. programming per the establishment cluster above. Fetch via `WebFetch` per the web-content policy.
- (The Apify Facebook Events scraper runs LIVE once a week on Mondays — see STEP 4 — and is a good source for small-town greater-MN leagues, local car-club shows, and Facebook-native events that lack a real website. On non-Monday runs it doesn't run, so on those days lean harder on the broad community/CVB/Chamber calendars and hyperlocal directories listed above.)

---

## STEP 3 — Compile data

### Data schema (19 fields, schema version 3.1):
title, description, category, price_type, price, age_range, address, latitude, longitude, image_url, image_source, date, time, phone, website, has_deal, deal_description, deal_expiry, tags

### `image_source` field: tags the provenance of `image_url`, so downstream consumers can distinguish a genuine event/venue photo from a generic website backfill or a topical stock image. Valid values:
- `facebook` — the image came from the Apify Facebook events scraper's `imageUrl` field (see STEP 4)
- `og_image` — the image was backfilled from a website's `og:image` meta tag AND passed the STEP 4.5 relevance check (a real event/venue photo or a self-branded image from the item's own dedicated/deep-link page — NOT a generic homepage/aggregator/umbrella-site banner)
- `site_photo` — a real photo pulled from the item's OWN page BODY (its largest non-logo hero/content `<img>`) in STEP 4.5, used when the page's `og:image` was absent or was only a logo/wordmark/banner. It clears the exact same STEP 4.5 relevance check as `og_image`, so it is an equally genuine, place-specific venue photo — every downstream step treats `site_photo` identically to `og_image` (never overwritten by a stock/curated_category fallback; the `curated` title/keyword override defers to it). Assigned in STEP 4.5.
- `stock_openverse` — the image is a topical, openly-licensed stock photo from Openverse, assigned in STEP 4.7 because no relevant `facebook`/`og_image`/`site_photo` was available (or the only website image found was a generic homepage/umbrella banner that STEP 4.5 demoted)
- `openverse_named` — a real photo of the item's *named* place/venue, pulled from Openverse by querying its proper name (e.g. "Minnehaha Falls Minnesota") and validated by distinctive-name-token overlap in the result's title/tags. Upgrades a generic `stock_openverse` (or blank) into an actual picture of THAT place when the item names a recognizable venue. Assigned in STEP 4.8.
- `curated` — a hand-picked image mapped to the item in `curated_images.csv` by an exact-**title** or **keyword** rule. It wins over every weak/fallback source (blank, `stock_openverse`, `openverse_named`, `curated_category`) and is the quality backstop for venues that have no photo of their own — but it **DEFERS to a genuine venue self-photo** (`facebook`, `og_image`, `site_photo`, `stock_openverse_specific`): a real, relevance-filtered photo of the actual place is left in place rather than overwritten with curated art (this is the helper's `REAL_PHOTO_SOURCES` guard, see STEP 4.8). These are the marquee-venue overrides the user maintains by hand. Assigned in STEP 4.8.
- `curated_category` — a hand-picked, topical *fallback* image mapped in `curated_images.csv` by a **category** rule (matches the item's `category`) or a **tag** rule (matches one of the item's `tags`). Unlike `curated`, this is **fallback-only**: it fills in for the weak auto-fallback sources (`stock_openverse`, `openverse_named`, or blank) but NEVER overrides a real `facebook`/`og_image` or a `curated` title/keyword pick. It's the reliable "at least a good topical image" backstop for the long tail that has no photo of its own. Assigned in STEP 4.8. Because it's generic (a category/tag image, not a photo of THIS place), it's a prime candidate for the fixer task to later upgrade to a real image.
- blank/empty — no `image_url` is set for this item (should be rare after STEP 4.7, which aims to leave nothing blank)

### Age-group tags: babies, toddlers, preschool, kids, tweens, teens, young-adults, all-ages, family
### Category tags: sensory-friendly, adaptive, dog-friendly, off-leash, county fair, art fair, fireworks, concert, outdoor movie, farm/u-pick, outstate/day-trip, sports, youth-sports, pro-sports, college-sports, game, tournament, rec, class, camp, lesson, clinic, registration, recurring, seasonal, ticketed, paid, free, family-dining, play-area, arcade, brewery-patio, kids-menu, kids-eat-free, character-dining, themed, dessert, car-show, cruise-night, parade, air-show, community-fest, touch-a-truck, craft-fair, food-truck, play-cafe, indoor-playground, tot-time, story-time, open-play, music-class, kids-gym, ecfe, teen-night, etc.
### City tags: duluth, rochester, st-cloud, mankato, moorhead, brainerd, bemidji, grand-rapids, winona, alexandria (omit for Twin Cities metro — that's the default). Note: `grand-rapids` = Grand Rapids, MN (Itasca County) — NEVER Grand Rapids, Michigan; drop any item whose location resolves to MI.

### Categories to produce:
1. **events** — All events TODAY through END_DATE statewide. Target 150+. Include date, time, website. Tag with city if outside Twin Cities metro. **This now includes sports (games, youth leagues, rec, tournaments, camps) and paid activities/classes** per the sports and paid-activity searches in STEP 2 — tag them with the relevant category tags (e.g. `sports`, `youth-sports`, `game`, `class`, `camp`, `ticketed`, `paid`, `free`) and set `price_type`/`price` accurately (free vs. ticketed vs. registration-fee). For recurring/seasonal items (leagues, classes, camps), use the start or registration date in `date` and describe the recurrence + any registration deadline in `description`. Aim for reasonable balance so no single type floods the list — roughly ≥15 sports/active-rec items and ≥15 paid activity/class/camp items per run when available, without crowding out the core community events.
2. **parks** — Carry forward the existing evergreen list (see the IMPORTANT note above on where to find it) — do not regenerate from scratch — just add any NEW parks discovered during searches. Parks outside Twin Cities metro are already tagged by city. **Dedup check (required every run):** before appending new discoveries, check whether any park (existing or newly found) is a near-duplicate of another entry — same official website/park-ID (e.g. two rows linking to the same `dnr.state.mn.us/state_parks/park.html?id=...`), or same address/near-identical lat-lon (within ~0.01 degrees) under a different name variant. If found, merge into a single entry rather than keeping both: prefer the officially-recognized name (e.g. the name used by the source agency/website) as the title, combine the descriptions/tags from both rows, and keep the more complete address/coordinates. Do this proactively — don't wait for someone to notice and report it. (This park-specific check is folded into and executed by the unified **STEP 4.3 precedence & de-duplication pass**, which also makes any must-include neighborhood-park seed the winning row of its cluster; it does not need to run as a separate step.) **Legacy carried-forward rows:** if any evergreen park row was written before schema v3.1 and thus has a populated `image_url` but a blank `image_source`, retroactively backfill `image_source` for it (typically `og_image`, since the evergreen list has never used the Facebook scraper) rather than leaving it inconsistent — do this as part of the normal carry-forward pass, not as a one-off.
3. **meal_deals** — Kids-eat-free and family meal deals valid on DAY_OF_WEEK. Search Twin Cities + add any found in other cities. Include website.
4. **volunteer_opportunities** — Family-friendly volunteer opps across Minnesota, TODAY through END_DATE.
5. **restaurants** — Family-friendly dining destinations statewide. Target ~25–30. This is broader than just deals: prioritize places that are a *draw* for families because of what kids can do or experience there — an indoor play area or playground, an arcade or games, themed/character dining, an attached mini-golf/entertainment center, or a standout kids' menu — as well as the classic teen-friendly and dog-friendly-patio spots. Primarily Twin Cities but include a few standouts per outstate metro. In `description`, name the specific kid draw. If the venue also runs a deal (e.g. kids eat free on a given day), record it inline via `has_deal`/`deal_description`/`deal_expiry` on the SAME row rather than only in meal_deals — a place can be both an experience and a deal. Tag each with the relevant dining tags (e.g. `family-dining`, `play-area`, `arcade`, `kids-menu`, `kids-eat-free`, `character-dining`, `themed`, `dessert`). (Do NOT scrape breweries/taprooms — see the exclusion rule in the family-dining search section; the only brewery-class venues in the guide are the three kept seeds.)

---

## STEP 4 — Apify Facebook scraper (LIVE — weekly, Mondays only)

**Run gate:** run this step ONLY when `RUN_APIFY` is True. **As of 2026-08-29 `RUN_APIFY` is hard-set to False (PAUSED by the owner until benefit is proven — see STEP 1), so this step is skipped on EVERY day, including Monday.** When skipped, do NOT call the Apify actor and do NOT produce `image_source: facebook` items — log a single `pipeline` `info` row (issue_type `fb_pull_skip`, item `apify_paused_0829`) so the skip is visible, and proceed straight from STEP 3 to STEP 4.3. (Formerly Monday-only; reinstated 2026-08-22 after being retired 2026-08-01; see STEP 1 for the full history, the pause rationale, and the cost cap.)

**What this step does (Mondays):** call the connected Apify Facebook Events scraper, apply the quality filters below, and fold the KEPT items into `events` so they flow into the live feed. This is the source of Facebook-native, no-website family events the STEP 2 calendar/CVB/library feeds miss. It replaces the old read-only shadow trial (STEP 6.5), which is now dormant — do NOT also run STEP 6.5 on the same Monday (that would call the paid actor twice; see the double-spend note in STEP 1).

**How to run it (Mondays only):**

1. **Call the actor with a cost cap.** Call the connected **`apify/facebook-events-scraper`** actor **via the Apify MCP (NOT WebFetch/bash)** with `maxEvents` = **`APIFY_MAX_EVENTS` (250 — the STEP 1 cost cap; a single TOTAL cap for the whole run, NOT per query)** and the `searchQueries` list below. **Query ordering matters — the list is sorted metro-outward by distance:** because `maxEvents` is a single total budget consumed as the actor works through the list in order, the queries are ordered nearest-first (metro core → inner suburbs → outer suburbs → greater-MN metros by driving distance from the Twin Cities), so if the budget runs short it is the farthest-flung, smallest towns that get sampled last, never the metro. The metro core and all four suburban rings (**west / north / south / east metro**, added 2026-08-22) sit up front; the three broad catch-all queries are pinned at the very END (an intentional exception to pure distance ordering — they're non-specific, so they should mop up any leftover budget rather than eat it before the named cities are reached). Full list:
   `["Minneapolis Minnesota family", "St Paul Minnesota family", "Minneapolis kids event", "St Paul kids event", "Bloomington Minnesota family", "Eagan Minnesota family", "Maplewood Minnesota family", "Minnetonka Minnesota family", "Eden Prairie Minnesota family", "Plymouth Minnesota family", "Woodbury Minnesota family", "Burnsville Minnesota family", "Blaine Minnesota family", "Maple Grove Minnesota family", "Coon Rapids Minnesota family", "Stillwater Minnesota family", "St Cloud Minnesota kids", "Mankato Minnesota family", "Rochester Minnesota family", "Winona Minnesota family", "Brainerd Minnesota family", "Alexandria Minnesota family", "Duluth Minnesota family", "Grand Rapids Minnesota family", "Bemidji Minnesota family", "Moorhead Minnesota family", "Twin Cities family event", "Twin Cities kids eat free", "Minnesota family festival"]`.
   (Ordering reference — approx. driving miles from Minneapolis. **Metro core:** Minneapolis/St Paul (0). **Inner-to-outer suburbs (~10–25 mi), by ring:** south = Bloomington, Eagan, Burnsville; east = Maplewood, Woodbury, Stillwater; west = Minnetonka, Eden Prairie, Plymouth; north = Blaine, Maple Grove, Coon Rapids. **Greater-MN metros, nearest-first:** St Cloud 65, Mankato 80, Rochester 85, Winona 120, Brainerd 130, Alexandria 135, Duluth 150, Grand Rapids 180, Bemidji 215, Moorhead 230. Suburban anchors are representative, not exhaustive — the MN-geo guard in step 3 still catches any out-of-state "Bloomington"/"Stillwater"/"Winona" collisions.)
   **Cost cap (rule):** you MUST pass `maxEvents = 250` and MUST NOT set it per-query or leave it empty (empty = unlimited = unbounded cost). ⚠️ Remember `maxEvents` is SOFT — the actor can overshoot it (the 2026-08-22 probe returned 121 for a cap of 30) and no hard per-run dollar cap is reachable through the MCP, so the account-level monthly hard usage limit (Console → Billing → Limits) is the real backstop (STEP 1). Then read the run's dataset items.
   **Resilience:** if the actor errors, times out, is not connected, or returns nothing, log a `pipeline` `info` row (issue_type `fb_pull_skip`, item = the reason) and carry on to STEP 4.3 — NEVER a hard failure, and never let this delay or block the rest of the pipeline. If it returns zero events on several consecutive weekly runs, log a `warning` `pipeline` row (issue_type `fb_pull_zero`) so a re-lockdown of Facebook search (like the one that caused the 2026-08-01 retirement) is caught.

2. **Post-run cost check (required):** after the run reaches a terminal state, call `get-actor-run` and read the actual events-extracted count (and charge, if the run stats expose it). Compute est. cost = events_extracted × $0.013. Log a `pipeline` `info` row (issue_type `fb_pull_cost`, item = est. cost + event count) every run so spend is tracked over time. If that est. cost exceeds `APIFY_MAX_USD` (5.00) — which the `maxEvents = 250` cap should prevent, but guards against the soft-cap overshoot, a pricing change, or the actor ignoring the cap — log a `warning`-severity `pipeline` row (issue_type `fb_pull_cost_exceeded`, item = the est. cost + event count) so an overspend is caught immediately, and drop `APIFY_MAX_EVENTS` for the next run.

3. **Filter every returned item (drop the junk; keep the genuine upcoming MN family events).** Apply these filters in order and DISCARD any item that fails one — only survivors move on to mapping in step 4:
   - **Date window:** drop if `isPast: true` or the start date is outside `[TODAY, END_DATE]`.
   - **MN-only geo guard:** drop if the `location` city/name resolves to any non-MN state. Apply the known traps explicitly: "Grand Rapids" pulls Grand Rapids **Michigan**; "Winona" pulls Winona in **GA/MS/other states**; "Twin Cities"/metro queries have pulled Saginaw MI, Sioux City IA, Spokane WA. Any non-MN resolve is dropped.
   - **Host-type filter (the private-event guard the user asked for):** determine whether the event is hosted by a Facebook **Page** (venue, museum, library, park, city, business, org, public figure) or by an **individual personal profile**. Genuinely private/invite-only events do not surface in anonymous public search, so the real risk is public-but-personal items — those are almost always personal-profile-hosted. **Drop personal-profile hosts.** If host type can't be determined, drop it too (do NOT let unknown-host items into the feed — when in doubt, leave it out).
   - **Denylist:** drop garage/estate/rummage/moving/"multi-family" sales, truckload/produce/meat fundraiser sales, gun shows, private "members/family only" reunions, birthday/wedding/funeral/celebration-of-life, MLM/vendor-recruitment/"try it" pitches, adult-only/21+/bar-crawl events.
   - Everything that survives all of the above is a KEEP. (Expect roughly a 1-in-5 keep rate — the 2026-08-22 probe kept ~20% after these filters.)

4. **Map KEPT items into the `events` schema and merge them in.** For each kept item, map the actor's fields to the STEP 3 schema: `name`→`title`, `description`→`description`, start date/time→`date`/`time`, `location` (venue name + address)→`address` (and `latitude`/`longitude` if present), `url`→`website` (a real `facebook.com/events/<numeric-id>` URL), `imageUrl`→`image_url` with **`image_source: facebook`**. Set `category` = `events` (route obvious dining/kids-eat-free deals to `meal_deals` and tag them, consistent with the rest of the pipeline), `price_type` = `free` unless the item clearly states a price, and add descriptive `tags`. Fold these rows into the compiled `events` dataset BEFORE STEP 4.3 so the precedence/de-duplication pass runs over them too — this is what prevents a Facebook row from duplicating a seed venue or a calendar-sourced event (STEP 4.3 rules handle the collapse; must-include seeds still win).

5. **Yield log (STEP 7):** log one `pipeline` `info` row (issue_type `fb_pull_yield`) summarizing the run: total pulled, total kept, dropped split by reason (`past_or_out_of_window` / `out_of_state` / `personal_host` / `host_unknown` / `denylist`), and of the kept how many were **net-new** (not already present in `events` before this step) vs. duplicates that STEP 4.3 later collapsed. This is the running record of whether the weekly scraper is earning its per-run cost.

---

## STEP 4.3 — Precedence & de-duplication (must-include seeds win; collapse duplicate entities) — REQUIRED

**Purpose:** by this point the compiled data holds everything from STEP 1–3 (web/aggregator searches), the STEP 2 must-include seed lists, and the STEP 4 Facebook results, all merged together. Two problems can quietly ride in: (a) a broad search independently rediscovers a venue/park that's ALSO on a must-include seed list, producing two rows for the same place; and (b) two different sources describe the same event/venue under slightly different names. This pass runs ONCE here — after all sources are merged, before the STEP 4.4 checkpoint build — so BOTH the checkpoint feed and the final feed come out clean. It has two jobs: **guarantee the must-include seed rows win**, and **collapse duplicate rows of the same entity into one**.

#### STEP 4.3a — adult / non-family programming exclusion (`adult_events_filter.py`) — REQUIRED, runs FIRST in 4.3

Library and community-center calendars publish **adult-services programming** into the same
feeds we harvest for family events — job-search and career-services help, Medicare/MNsure
counseling, 1:1 tech help for seniors, small-business support, "Employer of the Day" tabling.
These are not family/kid events and must not reach the guide. On the 2026-08-25 live feed there
were **230 such rows (55 unique titles)**. Run this FIRST in 4.3 — before the precedence/dedup
rules and before `dedup_titles.py` — so we don't spend dedup effort on rows we're about to drop:

```bash
python3 adult_events_filter.py _compiled_work.json --dry-run   # report only, changes nothing
python3 adult_events_filter.py _compiled_work.json             # apply; prints every drop
python3 test_adult_events_filter.py                            # must report 70 passed, 0 failed
```

Four rules that keep this from silently deleting real family events (same precision-over-recall
asymmetry as `dedup_titles.py` — **a wrong DROP deletes a real event; a missed one is only
cosmetic**):

1. **Match the TITLE only, never the description.** A community festival ("Fiesta Latina")
   whose *description* mentions a job-resource booth must survive. Description matching produced
   exactly that false positive in testing, so the helper reads titles only.
2. **Two tiers.** `DROP_PHRASES` are unambiguous adult-service titles that essentially never
   name a kids' event (job search, career services, medicare, mnsure, tech help, resume,
   small business support, employer of the day, community partner of the day, …). `REVIEW_PHRASES`
   are borderline (`senior center`, `caregiver support`, `ged`, `esl class`, …) — written to the
   report for a human, **never auto-dropped**. On live data this leaves 7 rows in REVIEW.
3. **Events category only, and never a seed.** The helper only touches `events` rows; parks /
   restaurants / meal_deals / volunteer are untouched. A row carrying `is_seed = true` is never
   dropped — if a seed matches a drop phrase it's sent to REVIEW as a collision instead.
4. **Word-boundary matching.** Phrases match on word boundaries, so a short token like `ged`
   does **not** fire inside `unplugged` or `Ridgedale`. `résumé` normalizes to `resume` and
   still matches. The helper is idempotent (re-running drops nothing) and accepts either the flat
   list or the `{category: [rows]}` shape.

Every drop and every review row is written to `_adult_filter_report.json`. In STEP 7, emit one
`pipeline` `info` row (issue_type `adult_event_filtered`) with the dropped and review counts, so
the exclusion is visible each run; if the DROP count ever spikes far above the ~230 baseline,
that's a signal a new source is flooding adult programming and the phrase list needs a look.

**`adult_events_filter.py` and `test_adult_events_filter.py` are fetched fresh from the GitHub
repo at runtime like the other helpers — a change to either only takes effect once committed to
`avlhohn/msp-family-feed`.** After any edit to the helper, re-run the test; it must report
`70 passed, 0 failed`. Every case in it is a real observed title, and the must-KEEP cases
(Fiesta Latina, caregiver-attended storytimes, kids' book clubs) outnumber the drops on purpose.

**Emit seeds first, and mark them.** When building the compiled dataset, emit every must-include seed row BEFORE folding in the free-search results, and carry an internal `is_seed = true` marker on those rows (the marker is for dedup bookkeeping only — do NOT emit it into the published JSON/CSV schema). This makes the seed the anchor row of any duplicate cluster it belongs to. **"Every must-include seed row" means EVERY `### Must-include …` section in this task — no exceptions.** This is category-agnostic: any row that originated from a must-include seed list is a seed and must be marked, regardless of which category it routes to. The current seed sections are: play cafés & coffee shops, game & entertainment venues, indoor playgrounds, kids' experience stores, candy & sweet shops, ice cream & treat shops, kid-friendly restaurants, multicultural kid-friendly restaurants & global food halls, children's & science museums, history museums & MNHS historic sites, marquee attractions, kids' theaters, apple orchards & farms, toy & experience stores, indoor adventure & trampoline parks, community centers & public indoor pools, standing attractions & water destinations, recurring meal-deal venues, and neighborhood parks. **If a new `### Must-include …` section is ever added below, it is automatically covered by this rule — mark its rows `is_seed = true` too, without needing this list re-edited.** A seed that carries a baked inline `Coordinates:` value (the museums, attractions, theaters, orchards, toy/experience stores, adventure parks, candy shops, and community centers/pools all do) is especially important to mark, because its coordinates are what let rule (3) below collapse a scraped twin that lands within ~150 m.

**How to decide two rows are the same entity (a duplicate):**
- **Normalize first.** Lowercase the title; **replace `&` with the word `and`** (so "Bar & Grill" and "Bar and Grill" normalize identically); strip punctuation and accents (café→cafe); drop trailing legal/common suffixes (`llc`, `inc`, `co`, `company`) and a leading `the`; collapse whitespace. **Strip a trailing location qualifier from the title before comparing** — anything after a " - " / " — " / "–" dash that is a city/region/neighborhood ("Maple Maze **- Maple Grove**", "Green Mill **- Duluth**", "Jersey's Bar and Grill **- Inver Grove Heights**") and any trailing parenthetical qualifier ("Nurture to Play - Sartell **(St. Cloud area)**"). These qualifiers are how the same venue arrives under three different titles; drop them so the core name lines up. Normalize the address to `<street-number> <street-name>` (lowercased, `st/street`, `ave/avenue`, `n/north` unified) and the website to its bare host (drop `www.`, scheme, path).
- **Build a title token-set for matching, and match on it — not just substring containment.** After normalizing, split the title into word tokens and drop (a) the joiner words `and`/`the`/`of`/`at` and (b) generic venue-type words that carry no identity: `bar`, `grill`, `pub`, `eatery`, `cafe`, `restaurant`, `kitchen`, `ristorante`, `pizzeria`, `company`, `co`. What remains is the identity token-set (e.g. "Jersey's Bar & Grill" → `{jerseys}`; "Green Mill Restaurant & Bar" → `{green, mill}`). **Two titles "match" when their identity token-sets are equal, OR one is a subset of the other (with the smaller set non-empty), OR they share ≥ 2 identity tokens.** This token-subset test is what a plain substring-containment check misses: an inserted middle word ("Jersey's Bar **and** Grill" vs "Jersey's Bar Grill") or an appended deal phrase ("Jersey's Bar & Grill **Thursday Kids Eat Free**") breaks substring containment but leaves the identity token-set intact. Wherever a rule below says "normalized-title exact-match or containment," this token-set match satisfies it. **Guard against a bare single-token collision:** if the shared identity token-set is a single very common word (e.g. `{park}`, `{grill}`, `{pizza}`), require another signal (address, coordinates, or website host) before merging — do not merge two venues on a lone generic token alone.
- **A "street address" for matching means a real street number + street name.** A bare city/region string ("Woodbury, MN", "Bloomington, MN 55425", "Twin Cities metro") is NOT a street address — normalize it to a city-only token and treat it as EMPTY for the address-match rules below. This matters especially for suburban venues, whose addresses often arrive as city-only until the STEP 4.6 website/enrichment step fills the street. Two rows that share only a city (or city+ZIP) are NOT duplicates on address alone, and a difference of only a trailing ZIP ("Burnsville, MN" vs "Burnsville, MN 55337") must NOT be read as two different addresses — both are city-only and neither can carry the address rule.
- **A duplicate is a pair of rows in the SAME category that match on ANY of:** (1) same normalized website host AND same city (use this instead of street when the street is city-only — a shared official website host plus the same city is a strong signal for the same venue); (2) same normalized *street* address (real number + street) — the strongest location signal, but only when both rows actually have a street number; (3) coordinates within ~150 m (≈0.0015°) of each other AND a normalized-title exact-match or containment (shorter side ≥ 8 chars); (4) normalized-title exact-match or containment (shorter side ≥ 8 chars) AND same city — the fallback that catches city-only-address near-duplicates like two "Grand Slam ... Burnsville" rows that rules 1–3 miss; (5) for parks specifically, the STEP 3 park rule also applies (same official park-ID/website, or same address / lat-lon within ~0.01°) — fold that check in here rather than running it separately. **Guard against the opposite error:** never merge on city alone — rule (4) additionally requires the title match, so two genuinely different Woodbury venues (e.g. "Slick City" and "Chuck E. Cheese", same city, different titles) stay separate.
- **Chains and multi-location brands are NOT duplicates of each other.** Parkway Pizza, Green Mill, Red Cow, Sebastian Joe's two cafés, Grand Ole Creamery's two shops, The Burrow's two sites — each *location* is its own row and must survive. The address/coordinate component of the match above is what keeps them separate: only merge rows that share the SAME location, never two rows that merely share a brand name. **Deliberate exception — a single chain-wide deal seed emitted as ONE consolidated row.** When a seed venue's entry explicitly instructs a CONSOLIDATED single-row emission for a shared multi-location deal (currently **Pizza Luce** — see its STEP 2 seed), that is intentional: emit exactly one `restaurants` row and one `meal_deals` row for the whole chain (all locations named in the `description`), and do NOT let a per-location scraped Pizza Luce hit re-split it back into 9 rows — treat any scraped Pizza Luce row as a duplicate of the consolidated seed and drop it.
- **Meal-deals / deals are keyed on venue + recurring day, NOT on deal wording.** For the `meal_deals` and `deals` categories, the same venue is repeatedly rediscovered with the deal phrased differently every time ("Kids eat free with purchase of adult meal" vs "Kids eat free with paid adult entrée" vs "one free kids meal per paying adult") — these varying `deal_description` strings are the SAME deal and must NOT be treated as different. Decide same-venue exactly as above (rules 1–4: website host + city, street address, coords ~150 m + title token-match, or title token-match + same city). Then apply the **recurring-day test**: extract the day-of-week the deal runs from the title, `deal_description`, and `tags` (e.g. "Thursday", "Sundays", "Monday Family Night"). **If both rows name a day and the days DIFFER, they are two distinct deals at the same venue — keep both separate** (e.g. Broadway Pizza's Monday Family Night vs its Thursday kids-eat-free are NOT duplicates). **If the days match, or one/both rows have no identifiable day, and they are the same venue, they ARE duplicates — merge them** (this is the Jersey's Bar & Grill case: three Thursday rows under three title/host variants collapse to one). When merging, prefer the row with the most specific street address and real coordinates as the survivor, and keep the clearest single `deal_description`. Do not merge a single-location deal row into a chain-wide "Multiple locations" umbrella row and thereby lose the specific location — if a specific-location row and an umbrella row for the same brand both exist, keep the specific-location rows (the umbrella is redundant once locations are enumerated; drop the umbrella only if every location it covers already has its own row, otherwise keep the umbrella and the enumerated locations both).
- **Events are dated occurrences.** For the `events` category, treat rows as the same only when they share a matching normalized title (token-set match, per the normalization bullet) AND the same `date` AND the same venue/location — OR when they share the **same event URL / registration deep-link** (bare-host + path match) AND the same `date`, which catches the same listing scraped under two different display names. When both rows carry a start `time`, a matching date **and** venue **and** title-token-set collapses them even if one title appends a promo tag ("Minnesota Twins vs White Sox" vs "SpongeBob Day at Target Field", same game/date/venue — same event). **False-merge guard for events:** if two rows share only a venue and date but have DIFFERENT start times, no title-token overlap, and no shared URL, they are different programs at the same place (e.g. a morning storytime and an evening concert) — keep them separate. The SAME venue on a DIFFERENT date is a legitimate separate row (recurring series) — never merge across dates.
- **Do NOT merge across the intentional venue-vs-programming split.** A play café's `restaurants` row and its open-play/story-time `events` rows describe different things and live in different categories — they are not duplicates. Dedup operates within a single category.

**What to do with a duplicate cluster:**
- **If the cluster contains a seed row (`is_seed = true`):** the seed row WINS — keep it, drop the scraped duplicate(s). But first salvage any field the seed row is missing by copying it over from the scraped row: a real `image_url`/`image_source`, a live `website` if the seed lacked one, a currently-running deal (`has_deal`/`deal_description`/`deal_expiry`), or an event `date`/`time`. Never let a scraped row overwrite the seed's curated `title`, `address`, routing (`category`), or `tags` — those are the hand-maintained values that are the whole point of the seed list. If two seed rows somehow collide, keep the more complete one.
- **If the cluster has no seed row (two scraped rows):** keep the more complete row (more populated fields: has coordinates, has website, has image, longer description) and merge the other's non-empty unique fields and tags into it, then drop the emptier row.
- **Log every merge** to STEP 7 as a `pipeline`-category `info` row, issue_type `duplicate_flagged`, naming the kept title and the dropped title(s) and which rule matched (e.g. "seed override: kept 'Sebastian Joe's Ice Cream — Linden Hills' (seed), dropped scraped 'Sebastian Joes' matched on address"). For parks this replaces/satisfies the STEP 3 "park entries merged" logging.

#### STEP 4.3b — title-variant pass (`dedup_titles.py`) — REQUIRED, runs LAST in 4.3

**Every rule above blocks on an exact normalized title.** Rules 1–5 group candidate rows by
normalized title first and then ask whether the *place* matches. That is the right shape for
"two sources found the same venue," and it is structurally blind to the opposite case: **same
place, same date, title phrased differently.** On 2026-08-22 that blindness shipped a visible
duplicate to the live app — two cards for *Live at the Rog: Alligations*, same Aug 23 6:00 PM,
same Frank Rog Amphitheatre, one title carrying a trailing `- Roseville` and the other not.
Line 901 does say to strip a trailing location qualifier, but nothing in the pipeline actually
executed it, and 216 such pairs were sitting in the compiled feed.

`dedup_titles.py` **inverts the blocking key**: it blocks on `(city, date)` and then compares
*titles*. Run it from the `Agents and Workflows` folder after the rules above have run:

```bash
python3 dedup_titles.py _compiled_work.json --dry-run   # report only, changes nothing
python3 dedup_titles.py _compiled_work.json             # apply; prints every merge
```

It collapses a pair only under **two narrow, named rules**, and refuses everything else:

- **`city_suffix`** — one title's token set is a strict subset of the other's, and *every*
  extra token is geographic (the resolved city name of either row, or `mn`/`minnesota`/
  `twin`/`cities`/`metro`/`area`). This covers `Maple Maze` ⇄ `Maple Maze - Maple Grove` and
  the prefix form `Day of the Dozers` ⇄ `Elk River Day of the Dozers`.
- **`reordered`** — the token sets are *equal* but the strings differ:
  `Children's Theatre Company: Pinocchio` ⇄ `Pinocchio - Children's Theatre Company`.

**Why there is no general subset rule.** A subset rule would merge `Minnesota Zoo` into
`Minnesota Zoo Wanderlight Trail`, and `State Fair - Alphabet Forest` into
`... Alphabet Forest Photo Booth` — an attraction eaten by a distinct attraction inside it.
A Jaccard-similarity rule was measured at 0.75 / 0.80 / 0.85 and **rejected for auto-merging**:
even 0.85 contained a false positive (`Duluth Children's Museum Harvest Festival` vs
`... Mini Harvest Festival`). *Mini*, *Junior*, *Preseason* and *Bar* occupy the same
syntactic slot as *Roseville*, and only the geographic ones are safely ignorable. **The
asymmetry is the whole design: a missed duplicate is cosmetic, a wrong merge silently deletes
a real venue or event from the guide.** Near-misses at Jaccard ≥ 0.8 are printed under
`REVIEW` for a human, never merged.

Three guards that must stay:

1. **Geography means the city name only, not the whole address.** An earlier version harvested
   every address token, which made neighborhood and street names droppable and merged
   `Red Cow - St. Paul` with `Red Cow St. Paul Cathedral Hill`, and `Colossal Cafe` with
   `Colossal Cafe Grand Avenue` — two locations of one chain collapsing into one, exactly what
   line 905 forbids.
2. **Differing start times block a merge**, and differing dates never share a blocking key.
3. **`parks` is exempt**, matching the existing exemption — distinct parks legitimately share
   a municipal address.

A known and *intended* quirk: an address of `Multiple locations, MN` resolves to the pseudo-city
`multiple locations`, which groups the chain-wide umbrella rows together — that is how a bare
`Pizza Ranch` umbrella row collapses into `Pizza Ranch - Multiple Minnesota Locations` while the
five real Pizza Ranch storefronts (each with its own city) stay untouched.

**Survivor and title choice.** The survivor is picked by `(is_seed, has_street_address,
field_count)` so seeds still win per the rules above, and the *richer* row's fields are kept —
but the **more specific title wins independently**: the survivor inherits the longer title
(`Maple Maze` → `Maple Maze - Maple Grove`) while keeping its own website, image and deal
fields. The log prints both original titles plus the final title, so a merge is auditable
after the rename.

**After any edit to `dedup_titles.py`, run `python3 test_dedup_titles.py`; it must report
`16 passed, 0 failed`.** Every case in it is a real observed row pair, and the must-not-merge
cases outnumber the merge cases on purpose.

**Result guarantee:** after this pass, every must-include seed venue/park appears exactly once (as its curated row, possibly enriched with a salvaged image/deal), and no two rows in any category describe the same physical place or the same dated event. STEP 8's `duplicate_flagged` count and the seed-presence check (issue_type `seed_venue_missing`) together make it visible every run that precedence and de-duplication actually fired.

**Fabricated-source guard — run at the very END of this pass, before the 4.4 checkpoint. REQUIRED, non-negotiable.**

A subagent asked to fetch a library feed can *invent* events rather than report them — most dangerously, it fabricates a plausible event **URL** by gluing together the title, city, and date instead of copying the feed's real ID. On 2026-08-19 the Scott/Carver/Anoka subagent did exactly this: it reported "Carver County Library: 86 in-window events" when the real feed had only a handful, and 44 fabricated Carver rows (URLs like `carverlib.libcal.com/event/familystory-chanhassen-sep05`) survived dedup and went live in the checkpoint feed. Counts were checked but contents were not. This guard makes that class of failure impossible to publish.

The tell is the URL shape, and it is deterministic. Booking-system event-detail pages carry a **machine ID**, never a human-readable slug:

- **LibCal** (`*.libcal.com/event/<id>`): the real `<id>` is **all digits** (e.g. `carverlib.libcal.com/event/17048265`). Any `.libcal.com/event/` URL whose id contains a **letter or a hyphen** is fabricated (e.g. `.../event/familystory-waconia-aug24`, `.../event/tailsreading-chaska-sep08`).
- **BiblioCommons** (`*.bibliocommons.com/events/<id>`): the real `<id>` is a **24-character hex string** `[0-9a-f]{24}` with no words or hyphens (e.g. `rclreads.bibliocommons.com/events/68c1c9a0f4e2b30012ab34cd`). Any bibliocommons `/events/` URL whose id contains a hyphen or dictionary word is fabricated.

Do this, programmatically (a regex pass over the compiled rows — do NOT eyeball it):

1. For every row whose `website` host ends in `libcal.com` or `bibliocommons.com`, extract the id after `/event/` or `/events/` and test it against the real-ID pattern above.
2. **Drop every row that fails** — a fabricated URL means the whole row is invented, not just its link. Do not try to "repair" it by blanking the URL; the title/date/venue are equally untrustworthy.
3. Log each drop to STEP 7 as a `warning`-severity `pipeline` row, issue_type `fabricated_url`, naming the title, the bad URL, and the source feed. Also emit one summary `warning` row (issue_type `fabrication_detected`) per feed that produced any fabricated row, with the drop count — this is the signal that a fetch subagent went rogue and its *other* output for that feed should be distrusted too.
4. **Cross-check against the feed's own claimed count.** For any LibCal/BiblioCommons feed where the number of rows that survived this guard is far below what the fetch subagent reported (e.g. it claimed 86 and 12 survive), do NOT accept the high count — re-fetch that one feed yourself with a raw oriented `WebFetch` and ingest only what you can see, exactly as the STEP 2 verification rule requires. A thin feed (Carver, Great River) outproducing a Hennepin-scale system is itself a fabrication red flag.

This guard is cheap, deterministic, and runs on the merged dataset, so it protects BOTH the 4.4 checkpoint and the final feed. It is the contents-level complement to the standing "never trust a subagent's counts" rule: here we distrust the subagent's *rows* and let the URL shape prove them real or fake.

**Prevention, upstream:** whenever a subagent is dispatched to read an iCal/LibCal/BiblioCommons feed (STEP 2 and its mandatory iCal pull), its prompt MUST include verbatim: *"Copy each event's URL and UID exactly as they appear in the feed's `URL:`/`UID:` field. NEVER construct, guess, or normalize an event URL from the title, city, or date. If an event has no URL in the feed, leave the URL blank — do not invent one."*

---

## STEP 4.4 — Early safety publish (feed checkpoint) — REQUIRED

**Purpose (this is the anti-timeout guarantee):** the image/website enrichment steps that follow (4.5, 4.6, 4.7, 4.8) are the slowest, most budget-hungry part of the whole run — dozens-to-hundreds of `WebFetch` calls and per-venue Openverse queries. If the run exhausts its time/turn budget partway through them, everything after (STEP 5 build, STEP 6 publish) never happens and the app is left serving a stale feed. To make that impossible, publish a COMPLETE feed on the core compiled data NOW, before any enrichment, as a safety net.

At this point the data already has every listing (all STEP 1–3 searches plus the STEP 4 Facebook results merged in). Images are mostly blank or `facebook`-only and some `website` fields are still empty — that's fine. A complete-listings guide with thinner imagery is vastly better than no fresh guide at all.

Do this:
- Run the STEP 5 build now on the current compiled data to emit `msp_family_guide.json` (the CSVs are written too, but you do NOT need to upload sheets here — see below).
- Publish ONLY the feed JSON to GitHub: perform the STEP 6 GitHub publish-and-verify sub-procedure exactly as written (fetch the token from the Drive file per STEP 6's Auth bullet, then GET-sha → PUT the base64-encoded JSON to the public repo `avlhohn/msp-family-feed`, path `msp_family_guide.json`, branch `main`, followed by the post-upload verify-and-repair loop). This guarantees a fresh, complete feed is live at the exact URL Base44 reads before enrichment starts. **Do NOT upload the checkpoint to Google Drive** — Base44 no longer reads Drive, so a Drive-only checkpoint would not protect the live feed.
- Do NOT publish the 5 category CSVs here — they are secondary human-facing views, not app-critical, and committing them twice per run would waste GitHub commits for no benefit. The category CSVs are published once, at the end, in STEP 6.
- **Non-fatal / never block on this:** if the build or feed upload fails here, log it (STEP 7, `pipeline` row, issue_type `feed_checkpoint`) and continue to the enrichment steps anyway — the checkpoint is a bonus safety net, not a gate. Log one `info` row (issue_type `feed_checkpoint`) on success too, so the run breadcrumb shows the checkpoint fired.
- Because GitHub commits to a single fixed path (`msp_family_guide.json`), the checkpoint and the final STEP 6 publish write to the SAME file — the final enriched copy simply overwrites the checkpoint through its commit, so there are no duplicate copies to reconcile. If the run dies during enrichment, the checkpoint commit remains the live feed on GitHub and keeps Base44 fully served with a complete (if thinner-imagery) guide.

---

## STEP 4.5 — Backfill images for non-Facebook items (og:image + body/hero photo)

> **These enrichment steps (4.5–4.8) are non-fatal UPGRADE passes.** A complete feed was already published in STEP 4.4, so if the run is interrupted or times out anywhere in 4.5–4.8, the app keeps serving that complete checkpoint feed — no run should ever end with nothing fresh published. Treat everything from here through STEP 5/6 as polishing that improves imagery and links on top of an already-delivered guide.

> **⏱️ HARD ENRICHMENT BUDGET (MUST ENFORCE — this is the anti-stall guarantee).** The whole enrichment block (STEP 4.5 through STEP 4.8) shares ONE wall-clock budget of **20 minutes**. At the very start of STEP 4.5, record `ENRICH_DEADLINE = now + 20 min` (capture it with a quick `date` call and do the arithmetic; keep the epoch value handy). Enrichment must NEVER run past this deadline. Enforcement rules:
> - **Dispatch backfill work in bounded WAVES, not one giant blocking fan-out.** Each wave is a small batch of `Agent` subagents (aim for ≤4 subagents, ≤10 items each, per wave). BETWEEN waves — never mid-wave — check the clock. If `now ≥ ENRICH_DEADLINE`, STOP dispatching new waves immediately and move on; do not start another wave hoping it'll be quick.
> - **Time-box every subagent you dispatch.** Include this instruction verbatim in each backfill subagent's prompt: *"You have at most 4 minutes. Return whatever you've confirmed so far as soon as you hit that limit — do NOT keep retrying slow or unresponsive pages. Partial results are expected and fine; never hang waiting on a single URL."* A subagent that returns partial or empty results is a success, not a failure.
> - **When the budget is exhausted (or a whole wave times out/errors), do NOT retry and do NOT wait — abandon the remaining backfill immediately.** Every not-yet-processed item is simply logged as its normal backlog row (`unresolved_image` for STEP 4.5, `unresolved_website` for STEP 4.6, respecting the OPEN-row dedup) so the `msp-family-guide-error-fixer` task picks it up later. Skipping enrichment for an item is a normal, expected outcome — the checkpoint feed already covers it and the fixer will upgrade it.
> - **After the budget check, ALWAYS fall through to STEP 5 → STEP 6 → STEP 7.** Reaching the final build, publishing the 5 category CSVs, and writing the error log are the steps that MUST run every day. Enrichment yields to them, never blocks them. If in doubt about time, cut enrichment short and publish.
> - STEP 4.8 (`image_upgrades.py`) has its own ~7-min internal timeout; if starting it would blow the shared 20-min budget, skip it (log issue_type `image_upgrade_skipped`) rather than letting it push the run past the deadline.


For every item across all 5 categories (events, parks, meal_deals, volunteer_opportunities, restaurants) that has a populated `website` field **but does not yet have a genuine venue photo** — meaning its `image_source` is blank, `stock_openverse`, `openverse_named`, or `curated_category` — attempt to backfill `image_url` by fetching the website and extracting a real photo of the place — **either** its `og:image` meta tag value **or**, when that tag is missing or is only a logo/wordmark/banner, the largest non-logo body/hero photo on the item's own page.

> **Why two candidates, not just og:image (fix adopted 2026-08-27).** `WebFetch` converts HTML→markdown through a summarizing model that routinely drops the `<head>`, so the `og:image` tag it's supposed to read survives only ~29% of the time even on pages that verifiably carry one — capture was empirically ~0 feed-wide despite ~66% of these pages actually having a usable photo. A one-time Chrome DOM read over 24 restaurant sites recovered a real photo on 21 of them; on roughly **half** the win came from `og:image` and on the other half from a prominent **body/hero `<img>`** because the site's og:image was a logo. Body images render as markdown `![alt](url)` and so *survive* WebFetch's conversion far better than the `<head>` tag does — asking for the body photo in the same call plays to WebFetch's strength and is what closes most of the gap. So STEP 4.5 now asks for BOTH in one fetch and keeps whichever passes the relevance filter, preferring a relevance-passing `og:image` and falling back to the body/hero photo.

> **⚠️ Eligibility is keyed on `image_source`, NEVER on an empty `image_url` (corrected 2026-08-28).** This step used to read *"has an empty `image_url` but a populated `website` field"*. That gate made STEP 4.5 structurally dead. STEP 4.8 fills EVERY remaining row with a `curated_category` image before publish ("290 missing images filled; 0 rows now ship without an image_url"), and the next run carries that filled feed forward as its base — so the eligible set was **exactly 0 rows** on every run after the first. Measured on the 2026-08-28 published feed: 5,195 rows, 3,920 with a `website`, **0 with an empty `image_url`**, and 3,389 website-bearing rows sitting on generic `curated_category` art. The 2026-08-27 og:image+body/hero widening was never reachable; it was not failing to capture, it was never eligible to try.
>
> This is the same trap as the `deal_source` and `is_seed` schema round-trips: **a within-run condition that a later step is designed to eliminate must never be used as an earlier step's entry gate.** If you ever find yourself writing "empty `image_url`" as the precondition here again, that is the bug.
>
> **Skip, do not re-attempt:** rows already labelled `facebook`, `og_image`, or `site_photo` — those are real photos and are finished. **Also skip `curated`** — those are the hand-maintained marquee overrides in `curated_images.csv`, and replacing one is the user's call, not this step's.

- **Apply the fixer's prior image resolutions FIRST:** before any og:image fetching, check the carried-forward `error_log.csv` (the same base described in STEP 4.6 and STEP 7) for a row matching this item where `issue_type` is `unresolved_image`, `resolved_date` is non-blank, and `resolution_note` contains a real image URL — a photo the `msp-family-guide-error-fixer` task already validated. If present, set the item's `image_url` to that URL and set `image_source` to the source the fixer noted (`og_image` or `openverse_named` as applicable, otherwise `og_image`), subject to the relevance filter below. Items that receive a usable prior resolution skip the og:image re-fetch. This keeps a photo the fixer already confirmed from being discarded and re-searched every run.
- Use the `WebFetch` tool only (never bash/curl/python-requests) to fetch each URL, per this account's web-content-restrictions policy. In the SAME fetch, ask the tool to return TWO things: (1) the `og:image` meta tag value if present, and (2) the URL of the single most prominent CONTENT photo on the page — a real scene/food/venue/interior photo — explicitly **excluding** logos, wordmarks, icons, favicons, social-share cards, sponsor/badge images, and nav/header/footer chrome. Have it return both URLs (either may be empty) so this step can choose between them below. Requesting the body photo is the point of the widening: it is the candidate WebFetch can actually see, since body `<img>` tags survive the markdown conversion that strips the `<head>`.
- **Bounded per-run quota, highest-yield first (added 2026-08-28).** The corrected gate exposes a backlog of roughly **3,400** eligible rows, while the shared 20-minute enrichment budget realistically supports only ~100–150 fetches per run. Do NOT try to drain it in one night. Each run take a bounded slice — target ~120 items, and stop at `ENRICH_DEADLINE` regardless — selected in this priority order: **(1)** rows **never attempted before** (see the attempt ledger below) ahead of previously-attempted ones; **(2)** within those, `restaurants` first, then `meal_deals`, `events`, `parks`, `volunteer_opportunities` — a one-time Chrome DOM probe recovered a usable photo on **21 of 24** restaurant pages, the highest-yield segment measured; **(3)** within a category, rows whose `website` is a **deep link** rather than a bare domain root, since the relevance filter demotes homepage banners anyway. Draining the backlog over roughly a month of nightly runs is the expected shape — a run that upgrades 100 rows is a success, not a shortfall.
- Given the volume (commonly 100+ URLs), parallelize this across multiple `Agent` (subagent) tool calls, each handling a batch of URLs, rather than fetching serially one-by-one. **Dispatch in bounded waves under the shared 20-min enrichment budget above, and give every subagent the 4-minute time-box instruction** — a hung venue page must never stall the run. Between waves, check the deadline; when it passes, stop and log the rest as `unresolved_image` backlog rows.
- **On no usable photo, PRESERVE whatever the row already had — never blank it (guard added 2026-08-28 alongside the widened gate).** If a page has no `og:image` tag AND no usable content photo, or the fetch fails: a row that arrived with NO image (truly empty `image_url`) is left empty; but a row that arrived carrying a `curated_category`, `stock_openverse`, or `openverse_named` image — which under the widened `image_source` gate is now the COMMON case, since nearly every eligible row already wears `curated_category` art — KEEPS its existing `image_url` and `image_source` untouched. STEP 4.5 may only ever UPGRADE a row to a real venue photo; a failed or empty-handed fetch must NEVER downgrade or blank an image the row already had, or the widened gate would strip a good-enough category image on every fetch that misses (and STEP 4.8, which would normally restore it, is skippable under the enrichment budget — so the blanked row could ship as-is). Do not fabricate a URL, and do not accept a logo/wordmark/banner just because it was the only image returned.
- Items with no `website` field at all (e.g. generic evergreen park entries) have no image source available; leave `image_url` empty for these and don't spend effort searching for one individually.
- **Choosing between the two candidates, and labeling the source:** apply the relevance filter below to each candidate, then keep the best surviving one. If a relevance-passing `og:image` exists, use it and set `image_source = og_image` (unchanged behavior). Otherwise, if the body/hero content photo passes the relevance filter, use it and set `image_source = site_photo` — a new source meaning "a real photo pulled from the item's OWN page body, when its og:image was a logo/banner or absent." Both `og_image` and `site_photo` are genuine, relevance-vetted venue photos and are treated identically by every downstream step (STEP 4.7/4.8 never overwrite them; the curated title/keyword override defers to them). If neither candidate survives the filter, apply the preserve-don't-blank rule above: a row that arrived empty stays blank (STEP 4.7 then gives it a topical stock image), while a row that arrived with a `curated_category`/`stock_openverse`/`openverse_named` image keeps that image and label unchanged — a failed upgrade never costs the row the image it already had.
- **Relevance filter (this is the key fix for "images not aligning with the events") — applies to BOTH candidates:** an image (whether the `og:image` or the body/hero photo) is only acceptable if it is plausibly a photo of *this specific* event/venue OR a self-branded image from the item's own dedicated site / deep-link page. DEMOTE (reject) images that are generic homepage or hero banners from aggregator, tourism-bureau, city-wide, library-system, chamber-of-commerce, or umbrella "what's-on" sites — these get reused across dozens of unrelated items and are the main cause of mismatched imagery. Signals to demote: (a) the `website` is a domain root/homepage or a shallow landing page rather than a deep link to this specific item; (b) the same image URL appears on many unrelated items; (c) the image is a logo, wordmark, or social-share card rather than a scene/photo. The body/hero candidate is held to the SAME bar — a hero image that is really a rotating aggregator banner or a sponsor logo is demoted exactly like an og:image would be; the body photo is only a win when it's a genuine scene/food/interior/venue shot on the item's own page. When both candidates are demoted, do NOT set `image_source` and do NOT clear an image the row already had: a row that arrived empty is left empty for STEP 4.7 to assign a topical stock image, while a row that arrived with a `curated_category`/`stock_openverse`/`openverse_named` image keeps it (per the preserve-don't-blank guard above — the demotion of a fetched candidate must never strip the fallback image the row came in with). Overall image preference order is: genuine `facebook` photo > deep-link/self-branded `og_image` > deep-link/self-branded `site_photo` (body/hero photo of the place) > `curated` (hand-picked title/keyword override) > `curated_category` (hand-picked topical fallback) > `openverse_named` (real photo of the named place) > topical `stock_openverse` > blank. **Correction (2026-08-28): `curated` sits BELOW the three real-photo sources, not above them.** This line previously led with `curated`, which contradicted both the `image_source` vocabulary earlier in this document and the shipped helper — `image_upgrades.py`'s `apply_curated_override()` carries a `REAL_PHOTO_SOURCES` guard and skips any row already labelled `facebook`, `og_image`, `site_photo`, or `stock_openverse_specific`. A real, relevance-vetted photo of the actual place beats generic curated art. Never keep a mismatched homepage banner just because it's technically a valid og:image tag or the biggest `<img>` on the page.
- **If subagent parallelization hits a rate limit or similar systemic failure mid-run** (forcing a fallback to a smaller manually-sampled subset, or skipping the step entirely), this is NOT something to silently absorb — log it as a `warning`-severity `pipeline`-category row in STEP 7's error log (issue_type `rate_limit`), including roughly how many of the eligible items were actually checked vs. the full eligible backlog, so a future run can resume the backlog rather than assuming today's yield reflects the true ceiling.
- **If the resulting image hit-rate for the run falls notably below the historical baseline**, log a `warning`-severity `pipeline`-category row (issue_type `low_hit_rate`) noting the actual vs. baseline rate and the likely cause if known. Note the baseline moved when the body/hero `site_photo` candidate was added (2026-08-27): the old ~7-8% figure reflected `og:image`-only capture, which was effectively ~0 in practice because WebFetch strips the `<head>`. With the body-photo candidate included, a healthy run should land materially higher (the one-time Chrome probe recovered a usable photo on ~21/24 restaurant pages), so recalibrate the "notably below" threshold against the combined `og_image`+`site_photo` yield after the first few post-fix runs establish the new normal — and count BOTH sources as hits, not just `og_image`.
- **Backlog logging (per-item):** for items that were NEVER attempted this run because of volume/rate-limit constraints (eligible under the `image_source` gate at the top of this step, but no fetch was even tried — note this condition was corrected on 2026-08-28 alongside that gate; it must NOT say "empty `image_url`" either), log each as an `info`-severity `unresolved_image` row in STEP 7's error log — one row per item — so the companion `msp-family-guide-error-fixer` task can pick up the backlog systematically. Before adding a row, check whether an existing OPEN row (same `item` + `issue_type: unresolved_image`, with `resolved_date` blank) already exists in the carried-forward log; if so, don't add a duplicate. **Attempt ledger — items that WERE attempted but yielded no usable photo (added 2026-08-28):** log each **once, ever** as an `info`-severity row with issue_type `attempted_no_photo` and `item` = the item title. **Leave `resolved_date`, `resolved_by` and `resolution_note` BLANK**, per the standing rule at the top of this document that this task never populates the three resolution columns — that is the fixer task's job alone. These rows do not become fixer work despite the blank `resolved_date`, because the fixer's queue selects only `issue_type` `unresolved_website` or `unresolved_image`; `attempted_no_photo` is not in that set and is invisible to it. The discriminator is the issue_type, never the resolution columns. Before writing one, check the carried-forward log for an existing `attempted_no_photo` row for the same item and do not duplicate it. This is deliberately NOT the daily-noise pattern the old wording warned against — it is written at most once per item, and its only job is to let the quota selection above skip pages already known to be dead so each run advances into fresh rows instead of re-grinding the same first ~120. **Without this ledger the corrected gate will re-fetch the same failures every night forever.** Re-attempt an `attempted_no_photo` item only if its `website` value has changed since the row was written, or if the row's `run_date` is more than ~90 days old (a site that had no usable photo last quarter may have one now).
- This step runs every day regardless of RUN_APIFY, since it's independent of the Facebook scraper.

---

## STEP 4.6 — Backfill missing website fields (verification-gated)

For every item across all 5 categories that has an empty `website` field, attempt to backfill it — but only with a verified, specific match. This step runs every day, independent of RUN_APIFY.

- **Apply the fixer's prior resolutions FIRST (so confirmed fixes actually reach the feed instead of being re-derived from scratch every day):** before dispatching any research, load the carried-forward `error_log.csv` (this is the same base loaded in STEP 7: read the session's `mnt/Agents and Workflows/error_log.csv` if present, otherwise fetch it from GitHub at `https://raw.githubusercontent.com/avlhohn/msp-family-feed/main/error_log.csv`). For each item that currently has an empty `website`, look for a matching row where `item` equals this item's title, `issue_type` is `unresolved_website`, `resolved_date` is non-blank, and `resolution_note` contains a URL — these are links the companion `msp-family-guide-error-fixer` task already confirmed. Apply that URL as the item's `website`, but re-validate it under the same verification standard below (a quick `WebFetch` confirming the page still loads and specifically names this venue/event); if it now fails to load or no longer matches, discard it and fall through to fresh research. Only items with no usable prior resolution proceed to the research batches below. This is what closes the loop: the fixer resolves an item once, and the fix stays live in the feed rather than being thrown away and re-researched daily.
- Batch the items needing a website (roughly ~10 per batch) and dispatch parallel `general-purpose` Agent subagents to research them. Each subagent may only use `WebSearch`/`WebFetch` — never bash/curl/requests. **This step shares the same 20-min enrichment budget as STEP 4.5 (the clock is not reset here) — dispatch in bounded waves, give every subagent the 4-minute time-box instruction, and when the shared deadline passes, stop and log the remaining items as `unresolved_website` backlog rows rather than waiting.**
- **Verification rule (strict):** a subagent may only return a URL if it can confirm the page specifically names or describes the exact venue/event/organization in question (matching title, and ideally address/city). If the match is ambiguous, generic (e.g. a sitewide homepage that doesn't clearly reference the specific item), or the agent isn't confident, it must leave the field blank rather than guess. Never fabricate or approximate a URL.
- Watch for address-field mismatches between this dataset and the real venue's official address (e.g. a park's listed address may differ slightly from the address on the venue's own site/city page) — this is a common reason a first-pass search fails to confirm a match; a follow-up search focused on the venue name + city (ignoring the dataset's address string) often resolves it.
- If a subagent batch leaves items unresolved, it's fine to do a small follow-up research pass on just those — don't force a match if genuine ambiguity remains (e.g. a business that may have closed, rebranded, or has a broken/expired domain — leave blank rather than linking a low-confidence guess).
- This same verification standard also applies any time a website is being added or corrected for an existing item (not just filling blanks) — never overwrite a working, specific website with a lower-confidence guess.
- **Every item left blank after this step must be logged** as an `info`-severity row in STEP 7's error log (category = that item's category, issue_type `unresolved_website`, item = the item's title), with a one-line description of why (ambiguous match, likely closed/rebranded with the rebrand name if known, broken/expired domain, etc.) so these can be reviewed and resolved by the companion `msp-family-guide-error-fixer` task without re-deriving the reasoning from scratch each time. Before adding a row, check whether an existing OPEN row (same `item` + `issue_type: unresolved_website`, with `resolved_date` blank) already exists in the carried-forward log; if so, don't add a duplicate — the existing open row already represents this issue.

---

## STEP 4.7 — Topical stock-image fallback (Openverse)

> **STEP 4.7's `empty image_url` gate is deliberately LEFT AS-IS (2026-08-28).** It looks like the same structural bug just corrected in STEP 4.5, and it has the same effect — `stock_openverse` is 0 feed-wide. Widening it would be a *downgrade*: a topical `stock_openverse` photo is weaker than the `curated_category` image these rows already carry, and STEP 4.8 would overwrite it anyway. On a carried base this step is correctly a near-no-op. **Do not "fix" it by symmetry with 4.5.**

After STEP 4.5, any item that STILL has an empty `image_url` — it never had a usable website image (neither a relevance-passing `og:image` nor a body/hero `site_photo`), or its only candidates were demoted as generic banners/logos in STEP 4.5 — should be given a topical, openly-licensed stock photo so the guide is never left with blank or mismatched imagery. This step runs every day, regardless of RUN_APIFY.

- **Source:** Openverse (`https://api.openverse.org/v1/images/`), which aggregates openly-licensed (CC / public-domain) images from Wikimedia Commons, Flickr, etc. Because Openverse is a public, read-only image-search API (not arbitrary web-page scraping), it may be queried directly (e.g. via a small Python helper), unlike event/venue web pages which must go through `WebFetch`.
- **Build a topical query per item** from its category + tags + title so the stock photo actually resembles the kind of item — not a generic filler. Examples: a splash-pad event → "children splash pad water play"; a state park → "minnesota state park forest trail"; a kids-eat-free deal → "family restaurant dining kids meal"; a volunteer cleanup → "volunteers community park cleanup"; a farmers-market event → "farmers market produce stall". Reuse a small, stable set of per-category/per-topic queries so results can be cached and not re-fetched per item.
- **Variety (important):** many items share the same topical query (e.g. dozens of "state park" rows). Fetch several results per query and distribute them **round-robin** across the items sharing that query — keep a per-query counter and rotate through the returned URLs — so the guide doesn't show the identical photo on 40 rows.
- **On success, set `image_source = stock_openverse`.** If Openverse returns nothing usable for a specific query, fall back to a small generic pool (e.g. "minnesota summer family outdoors", "community festival crowd", "family dining table") before giving up. Only leave `image_url` blank if even the generic pool is empty/unreachable — this should be rare.
- **Relationship to the fixer task:** a `stock_openverse` image is a *fallback*, not necessarily a perfect match. Items that received only a stock image (no real `facebook`/`og_image` was ever found) should keep their existing OPEN `unresolved_image` log row from STEP 4.5 so the companion `msp-family-guide-error-fixer` task can later upgrade them to a genuine photo if one appears — the stock image just ensures nothing displays blank in the meantime. Do NOT log a *new* `unresolved_image` row here if STEP 4.5 already logged one for that item (respect the OPEN-row dedup).
- **Logging:** if Openverse is unreachable or returns nothing for the whole run (systemic failure), log one `warning`-severity `pipeline` row in STEP 7 (issue_type `stock_unavailable`) with rough coverage numbers (how many items still blank vs. total attempted).

---

## STEP 4.8 — Named-entity + curated image upgrade

After STEP 4.7 every item has some image (real or stock). This step upgrades several classes of item, using the persisted helper `image_upgrades.py` and `curated_images.csv` in the current session's working folder. Run it as: `python3 image_upgrades.py <compiled_data.json> --curated curated_images.csv --deadline-seconds 420`, passing the path to the compiled-data JSON that holds the run's item list (the same data STEP 5's build reads). The Openverse named-entity layer has contributed 0 rows for several runs while timing out; pass `--no-openverse` to skip it (the Wikimedia layer below is the reliable real-photo source).
