The Wikimedia venue-photo layer is ON by default with its own `--wiki-deadline-seconds 240` budget; pass `--no-wikimedia` only to disable it.

> **MANDATORY — run the committed helper verbatim; do NOT hand-roll this step.** The curated overlay MUST be applied by executing `image_upgrades.py` (the committed file fetched from GitHub, see below) as a subprocess. Do NOT reimplement any of its matching, pooling, or image-assignment logic inline in Python, a heredoc, or by hand — even if it seems faster or the helper looks simple. The helper's whole value is the **pool distribution**: when several rows share one `tag`/`category` value it spreads them across items by a stable per-title hash so a page never shows the same picture on 40 cards. A hand-rolled version that stamps one image per tag/category value is the exact clustering bug this step exists to prevent, and it has shipped to the live app before. **The helper only ever writes four `image_source` values: `wikimedia`, `openverse_named`, `curated_category`, or `curated`. It NEVER writes `curated_tag` (or any other label). If you find yourself about to emit `image_source = "curated_tag"`, STOP — that proves you are reimplementing the step instead of running the helper. Run the helper.** (`wikimedia` is a legitimate real-photo label written by the venue-photo layer — see below — not a reimplementation artifact.) Likewise, STEP 5's `build_guide.py` must carry `image_url`/`image_source` straight through — it must never recompute or reassign images after this step. If you omit the data-file argument the helper auto-discovers the largest item-list JSON in the working folder, but passing it explicitly is preferred. The helper reads `curated_images.csv`, applies all three layers, and writes the upgraded data back to that same file only at the end. Give it a generous timeout (~7 min); it self-enforces the `--deadline-seconds` budget on its Openverse layer and fails soft on any network error (a single bad lookup never aborts the run). **This ~7-min step runs INSIDE the shared 20-min enrichment budget from the STEP 4.5 preamble — if starting it would exceed that deadline, skip it and log issue_type `image_upgrade_skipped` rather than blowing the budget.**

`curated_images.csv` has FOUR `match_type` values, in two precedence classes. **Overrides** (`title`, `keyword`) win over everything, including a real `facebook`/`og_image`; they're the user's marquee hand-picks. **Fallbacks** (`category`, `tag`) fill in ONLY for the weak auto-sources and never touch a real venue photo. Match semantics: `title`/`keyword` match on the item's title text (as today); `category` matches the item's `category` field exactly (one of `events`/`parks`/`meal_deals`/`volunteer_opportunities`/`restaurants`); `tag` matches when `match_value` is present in the item's `tags` list. Blank-`image_url` rows are ignored throughout.

Four layers, applied in this order:
- **Named-entity photo (openverse_named):** for any item whose `image_source` is blank or `stock_openverse` AND whose title names a recognizable proper place/venue (a state park, named lake, zoo, museum, landmark, named festival, etc. — NOT a generic "storytime" or "kids eat free" row), query Openverse by the proper name + " Minnesota", fetch several candidates, and pick the one whose title/tags share the MOST distinctive name tokens with the item. Reject person/portrait/protest shots. On a confident match, replace the image and set `image_source = openverse_named`. If nothing validates, leave the existing stock image untouched. (Currently skipped via `--no-openverse` — it has been a timing-out no-op; the Wikimedia layer replaces it as the real-photo source.)
- **Wikimedia venue photo (wikimedia):** the fix for the "web images never populate" ratchet. A named venue's row arrives from the carried base already stamped `curated_category` (a generic category image) from a prior run, and every other upgrade layer treats `curated_category` as final — so once frozen, a named place could NEVER get a real photo. This layer is deliberately eligible on `curated_category` **as well as** blank/`stock_openverse`/`openverse_named`, and resolves the venue's OWN photo from Wikipedia's REST summary API (`https://en.wikipedia.org/api/rest_v1/page/summary/<name>` — a direct read-only API, NOT scraping). Four precision guards, each catching a real observed failure: (1) a **place-hint gate** — only venue-shaped titles (containing park/zoo/museum/lake/nature center/… ) are queried, so generic activity titles like "Chess Club" are never attempted; (2) **name/page token overlap** — the returned Wikipedia page title must share a distinctive token with the venue name; (3) a **Minnesota bounding-box check** on the article's coordinates — this blocks a same-named place in another state (a live run matched "Crooked Lake Park" to a park in *Florida* and "Lakefront Park" to a defunct Chicago ballpark; both correctly rejected); (4) a **logo/wordmark/SVG screen** — a Wikipedia brand logo is not a venue photo (Mill City Museum, Somali Museum, Hmong Cultural Center and Hennepin History Museum resolved to logos and were dropped back to their curated fallback), the same standard as STEP 4.5's logo filter. Disambiguation pages and 404s resolve to nothing and the item keeps its fallback image. On a confident match, set `image_url` to the Wikimedia photo and `image_source = wikimedia`. It NEVER overwrites a real self-photo (`facebook`/`og_image`/`site_photo`) or a hand-picked `curated` override. The layer is time-boxed (`--wiki-deadline-seconds`, default 240), fails soft on any network error, is idempotent (a warm re-run changes nothing), and disk-caches every lookup by normalized venue name (`_wiki_cache.json`). On today's feed it moved ~50 event cards across ~29 marquee Minnesota venues (state parks, Minnesota Children's Museum, Science Museum of Minnesota, Children's Theatre Company, SEA LIFE, etc.) from a generic category image to the venue's own photo. **Cache warm-start (wired 2026-08-29):** the disk cache does not persist across daily sandboxes, so a cold run rebuilds it (~429 name lookups) and lands ~41 on the first pass, converging to ~50 only on a later run as transient errors clear. The helper now **self-primes**: on startup, if no local `_wiki_cache.json` exists, it fetches the published copy from `https://raw.githubusercontent.com/avlhohn/msp-family-feed/main/_wiki_cache.json` (a direct read-only raw fetch, no auth, soft-fail — a cold start is always safe, just slower) so a fresh sandbox starts WARM and hits ~50 in one pass. A local copy always wins (it is fresher); pass `--no-wiki-cache-fetch` to force a cold rebuild for testing. **You must then re-publish the freshened cache after the run** so the next day starts warm too — see STEP 6, where `_wiki_cache.json` is shipped alongside the six feed artifacts. To force a full refresh, delete the committed `_wiki_cache.json` from the repo.
- **Curated category/tag fallback (curated_category):** applied to every item whose current `image_source` is one of `blank`, `stock_openverse`, or `openverse_named` (i.e. it has NO real venue photo of its own — a `facebook`, `og_image`, or `site_photo` item is skipped and keeps its real photo). If such an item matches a `tag` row OR a `category` row in `curated_images.csv`, set its `image_url` to that hand-picked fallback and set `image_source = curated_category`. When an item matches BOTH a `tag` row and a `category` row, the **`tag` row wins** (more specific). This layer deliberately supersedes the flaky `stock_openverse`/`openverse_named` auto-matches with a reliable topical image, which is the whole point — a good generic beats a wrong specific. It does NOT override a real `facebook`/`og_image`/`site_photo`. **Multiple rows may share the same `tag` (or `category`) value to give a pool of images for that concept: the helper gathers every row for a matched value into a list and picks one per item by a stable hash of the item's title. This spreads many events carrying the same high-frequency tag (e.g. `parade`, `community-fest`, `festival`, `sports`, `music`, `car-show`) across the available images instead of stamping the identical picture on all of them, while keeping any single event on the same image run-over-run (no flicker). To add differentiation for a tag, just add more `tag,<value>,<image_url>` rows for it; one row still behaves exactly as before.
- **Curated title/keyword override (curated):** applied LAST. If the item matches a `title` or `keyword` row in `curated_images.csv`, set its `image_url` to that hand-picked URL and set `image_source = curated`. This wins over the weak/fallback sources (blank, `stock_openverse`, `openverse_named`, and any `curated_category` fallback) — it's the quality backstop for venues that have **no** real photo of their own. **But it DEFERS to a genuine venue self-photo: if the item's `image_source` is already `facebook`, `og_image`, `site_photo`, `stock_openverse_specific`, or `wikimedia`, leave that real photo in place and do NOT overwrite it with curated art.** Rationale: an `og_image` or `site_photo` only earns its label after STEP 4.5's relevance filter has already rejected logos, wordmarks, banners, and aggregator images, so by this point it is a vetted, place-specific photo of the actual venue — and a real photo of the place should beat a generic curated stand-in. (This is exactly what the committed `image_upgrades.py`'s `apply_curated_override` does via its `REAL_PHOTO_SOURCES` guard; the helper is the source of truth — do not reintroduce an "overrides everything" version inline.)

This step runs every day, regardless of RUN_APIFY.

**Always read `curated_images.csv` straight from the public GitHub repo (the committed copy is the source of truth) — do NOT trust any pre-existing local/working-folder copy, since a stale mirror may linger.** At run start, unconditionally re-fetch the canonical curated file from the raw GitHub URL:
- Fetch `https://raw.githubusercontent.com/avlhohn/msp-family-feed/main/curated_images.csv` (public repo — **no auth/token needed for reading**). To defeat CDN caching, append a cache-buster query param (e.g. `?t=<unix_epoch>`) and/or send a `Cache-Control: no-cache` header. **Overwrite** whatever `curated_images.csv` is in the session working folder with the fetched bytes. This guarantees the build always uses the freshest committed edits the user made, not a sync-stalled copy. A simple `curl -fsSL "https://raw.githubusercontent.com/avlhohn/msp-family-feed/main/curated_images.csv?t=$(date +%s)" -o curated_images.csv` works.
- **Freshness log (so stale-copy problems are visible):** read the fetched response's `Last-Modified` header, or query the commits API `https://api.github.com/repos/avlhohn/msp-family-feed/commits?path=curated_images.csv&per_page=1` for the last commit date. If that timestamp is OLDER than ~20 hours before this run's start (a sign the user's latest edits were never committed), log a `warning`-severity `pipeline` row in STEP 7 (issue_type `curated_stale`) noting the timestamp and byte size, so the user is alerted that the build may be using an out-of-date curated file. Still proceed with whatever the fetched copy contains.
- If the GitHub fetch itself FAILS (network error, or a non-200 response), fall back to any `curated_images.csv` already present in the working folder; if there is none, skip the curated layer (don't fail the run) and log a `warning`-severity `pipeline` row (issue_type `image_upgrade_skipped`).
- **Validate every curated `image_url` before applying it (a curated row is only useful if the URL is a real, directly-renderable image).** For each non-blank row, check the `image_url`: it should look like a direct image resource — an `http(s)` URL whose path ends in a real image extension (`.jpg`, `.jpeg`, `.png`, `.webp`, `.gif`, `.avif`), optionally followed by a query string, OR a known image-CDN/`googleusercontent`/`images.` style host that serves raw image bytes. A URL that points at a **web page** rather than an image (e.g. `https://www.walkerart.org/minneapolis-sculpture-garden/`, any path ending in `/`, `.html`, `.htm`, `.aspx`, or a bare domain) is NOT a valid curated image and will render as a broken image in Base44. When a curated row fails this check: do NOT apply it (leave the item's existing STEP 4.5/4.7/4.8 image in place rather than overwriting a working image with a broken one), and log a `warning`-severity `pipeline` row in STEP 7 (issue_type `curated_bad_url`) naming the row's `match_value` and the rejected `image_url`, so the user can fix that row in Drive. Rows that pass the check apply normally as the curated override.

**The helper `image_upgrades.py` is fetched only if missing** from the working folder — fetch it from the public GitHub repo (its durable home) at `https://raw.githubusercontent.com/avlhohn/msp-family-feed/main/image_upgrades.py` (no auth/token needed for reading; append a `?t=$(date +%s)` cache-buster) and write it into the working folder before running, e.g. `curl -fsSL "https://raw.githubusercontent.com/avlhohn/msp-family-feed/main/image_upgrades.py?t=$(date +%s)" -o image_upgrades.py`. If the helper is STILL missing after the GitHub fetch is attempted (network error / non-200), skip this step (don't fail the run) and log a `warning`-severity `pipeline` row in STEP 7 (issue_type `image_upgrade_skipped`), naming the file that couldn't be obtained.

### STEP 4.8 verification gate (declustering check — REQUIRED before STEP 5)

Immediately after the helper finishes, verify the overlay actually distributed images. This gate catches both a silently hand-rolled overlay AND a helper that ran but didn't pool. Compute two checks over the just-written data:

1. **Zero forbidden labels.** No item may have `image_source == "curated_tag"` (nor any label other than the real set: `facebook`, `og_image`, `site_photo`, `stock_openverse`, `openverse_named`, `curated_category`, `curated`, or blank). A single `curated_tag` means the step was reimplemented — it did NOT run the committed helper.
2. **No image dominates a category.** For each of the 5 categories, compute the share of items assigned the single most-common `image_url`. It must be at or below ~20%. Known-good reference shares from a correct helper run: events ~7%, parks ~12%, meal_deals ~13%, volunteer ~19%, restaurants ~10%. A category whose top image far exceeds ~25% did not get pool distribution.

3. **Real-photo coverage (observation — NON-blocking, added 2026-08-28).** Count items whose `image_source` is `facebook`, `og_image`, or `site_photo`, and record the number in the same `image_decluster_check` row. This is the single number that says whether STEP 4.5 is actually working. The 2026-08-28 baseline was **4** (all `og_image`, zero `site_photo`) out of 5,195 rows, against 3,920 rows holding a live `website`. It should climb run over run as the backlog drains. Do NOT fail the gate on a low count — one bad night of fetches is not a declustering failure — but a count that stays flat across several consecutive runs means STEP 4.5's eligibility gate has regressed, and that is the first thing to check.

If EITHER of the first two checks fails: re-fetch `image_upgrades.py` fresh from GitHub (cache-buster) and re-run it verbatim on the data once more. If it still fails, log an `error`-severity `pipeline` row in STEP 7 (issue_type `image_decluster_check`) recording, per category, the top-image share and any forbidden-label count — then proceed to STEP 5 with whatever the helper produced (never publish a hand-rolled overlay). If both checks pass, log an `info`-severity `pipeline` row (issue_type `image_decluster_check`, resolution note `passed`) with the per-category top-image shares so the healthy distribution is visible run-over-run.

---

## STEP 4.9 — Geocode coordinates for every mappable item (fill blank lat/lon)

Base44 drops a map pin for every item that carries a valid `latitude`/`longitude`. Historically nothing in this pipeline actually *geocoded* an address, so any row that arrived without coordinates (most scraped restaurants, deals, volunteer spots) showed up with no pin. This step closes that gap: after enrichment and BEFORE the final build, every item that has a usable address but blank/zero coordinates gets geocoded. This step runs every day, regardless of RUN_APIFY, and shares the same fail-soft philosophy as 4.5–4.8 (never let a geocode miss abort the run).

**What to geocode:** any item whose `latitude` OR `longitude` is blank, null, or `0` AND that has either a street `address` or a recognizable venue name + city. Items that already carry valid in-Minnesota coordinates (including seed restaurants populated from the baked-in Coordinates table, and all `parks.csv` rows that already have coords) are LEFT ALONE — do not re-geocode them.

**How to geocode (OpenStreetMap / Nominatim — same structured-data-API treatment as Overpass for parks):**
- Endpoint: `https://nominatim.openstreetmap.org/search?format=json&countrycodes=us&limit=1&q=<url-encoded query>`. **You MUST send a descriptive `User-Agent` header** (e.g. `msp-family-guide/1.0 (avlhohn)`) or Nominatim returns 403. Respect the ~1 request/second rate limit — sleep ~1.1s between calls. Query it with `urllib` in a Bash heredoc script (NOT WebFetch, NOT `requests`), exactly like the parks Overpass step. Remember the filesystem gotcha: create the script via `cat > file <<'PY'`, not the Write tool.
- **Query strategy (try in order, stop at first in-MN hit):** (1) full street address `"<street>, <city>, MN <zip>"`; (2) if that fails, name-search `"<venue name>, <city>, MN"` (this rescues highway-style addresses like "Hwy 29 S" / "State Hwy 13" that street-geocoding often can't resolve). 
- **Validate every result inside Minnesota's bounding box: lat 43.3–49.5, lon −97.5 to −89.4.** Reject anything outside it (a common failure mode — e.g. a same-named town in another state; the Maplewood/Osaka lookup once returned Maryland). A rejected result counts as a miss; fall through to the next query strategy.
- **City-centroid fallback:** if both address and name searches fail or fall outside MN, geocode just the city (`"<city>, MN"`) and use that centroid so the item still maps to roughly the right area. When you use the centroid fallback, note it (see logging) — the pin is approximate.
- **If even the city can't be resolved,** leave lat/lon blank (Base44 simply shows no pin — never fabricate a coordinate) and log it.

**Batch efficiency:** collect all to-be-geocoded queries and run them in one heredoc script that loops with the rate-limit sleep, writing results back to the compiled-data JSON that STEP 5's build reads. Cache within the run so identical `"city, MN"` centroids aren't looked up twice.

**Budget:** this step is quick relative to image enrichment, but it still shares the anti-stall spirit — cap it at ~5 minutes of wall-clock. If the list is huge and the cap is hit, geocode what you can (prioritize `restaurants`, then `events`/`meal_deals`, then the rest), leave the remainder blank, and move on to STEP 5. Never let geocoding push the run past its build/publish steps.

**Logging (STEP 7):** log an `info`-severity `pipeline` row summarizing the pass — how many items needed geocoding, how many resolved via street address, how many via name-search, how many via city-centroid fallback (issue_type `geocode_approximate`, list them), and how many were left blank because nothing resolved (issue_type `geocode_unresolved`, list them so the user can hand-fix or add a baked coordinate). DJ's Tap House (Alexandria) is the known highway-address case that runtime geocoding must handle here.

---

## STEP 5 — Generate build_guide.py and run it (final, enriched build)

This is the FINAL build, re-run now that enrichment (4.5–4.8) has upgraded imagery and websites — it supersedes the STEP 4.4 checkpoint feed. (If STEP 4.4 already wrote `build_guide.py`, just re-run it against the now-enriched data rather than regenerating it from scratch.)

Write `build_guide.py` (in the current session's own working directory) with all compiled data — including the `image_url` and `image_source` values populated in STEP 4, STEP 4.5, STEP 4.7, and STEP 4.8, the `website` values populated in STEP 4.6, the `latitude`/`longitude` values populated in STEP 4.9, and the precedence & de-duplication (seed-wins, entity-collapse, park merges) from STEP 4.3 plus the retroactive image-source tagging from STEP 3 — then execute it with Python. The script must:
- Output JSON to the current session's `mnt/Agents and Workflows/msp_family_guide.json`
- Output 5 CSVs: events.csv, parks.csv, deals.csv, volunteer.csv, restaurants.csv
- Include `metro_area`: "Minnesota Statewide (Twin Cities metro + Duluth, Rochester, St. Cloud, Mankato, Moorhead, Brainerd, Bemidji, Winona, Alexandria)"
- Include `schema_version`: "3.1"
- **If the script itself throws an error (bad data, malformed CSV row, etc.), do not just fix it silently and move on** — after fixing and successfully re-running, still log the original failure as an `error`-severity `pipeline`-category row in STEP 7's error log (issue_type `build_failure`) with the error message, so recurring root causes are visible over time even though the run still completed.

### STEP 5.5 — Stamp `end_date` on multi-day rows (JSON feed only)

Base44's Event entity has an `end_date` field and `dailyJsonImport` carries `r.end_date` (app-side "Fix 4", 2026-08-29). Our source rows have **no** structured end date — only a single `date` — so `end_date` is parsed from each row's own text by the committed helper **`end_date_pass.py`** (folder root). Run it **after** `build_guide.py` has written `msp_family_guide.json`, and **before** STEP 6 publishes:

```bash
python3 end_date_pass.py msp_family_guide.json --dry-run   # report; eyeball the sample
python3 end_date_pass.py msp_family_guide.json             # apply (writes end_date into the JSON)
python3 test_end_date_pass.py                              # must report 20 passed, 0 failed
```

Four rules this step depends on — all precision-first, because a **wrong** `end_date` asserts an event is still running when it isn't (the same failure class as the STEP 1 date-roll-forward rule):

1. **It only touches the JSON feed, never the CSVs.** `end_date` is deliberately NOT added to `build_guide.py`'s `BASE`/`COLS`, so the published **21-column CSV contract is unchanged** (same reason the `deal_source`/`is_seed` fields are kept out of `BASE`). `dailyJsonImport` reads the JSON, so the JSON is the only place the value must land. If you ever move `end_date` into the CSVs, confirm no CSV consumer breaks on a 22nd column first.
2. **It emits on only two self-anchored signals**, both anchored to the row's own start `date`: (a) a bare `YYYY-MM-DD` in title/description that is `> date` and `≤ 15 months` out; (b) a spelled `"through|until|ends <Month> <Day>[, Year]"` with a **numeric day**. Everything else stays blank — that is intentional. Vague forms with no day (`through winter`, `through end of September`, `through preschoolers`, `through late September`) match nothing; do not "improve" recall by loosening this — those exact phrasings are false positives in the test.
3. **Dated categories only** (`events`, `meal_deals`, `volunteer_opportunities`). `parks`/`restaurants` are evergreen (STEP 5 blanks their `date`), so they get no `end_date`.
4. **Idempotent and non-destructive** — it never overwrites an `end_date` already present. Expected yield on a normal feed is ~130 rows (75 ISO-anchored + ~58 spelled); a sudden drop to ~0 means the helper is stale or the feed shape changed — investigate rather than shipping a feed with no ranges.

---

## STEP 6 — Publish outputs to GitHub

Everything published in this step goes to the public GitHub repo **`avlhohn/msp-family-feed`** (branch `main`): the feed JSON that Base44 reads, AND the 5 human-facing category tables. Each output overwrites ONE fixed file in place, so nothing accumulates over time. **Google Drive is no longer written to in this step at all** — the old daily Google-Sheets copies (which used to pile up a new set every run in folder `1SlAJxG1H-T1-Dnsoy2HYmuOfOTZLmGwX`) are retired. Fetch the GitHub token once (see the **Auth** bullet below) and reuse it for every PUT in this step.

**Publish the 5 category tables to GitHub as CSVs — overwrite in place (this REPLACES the old Drive Google-Sheets upload):** after STEP 5 has written the 5 local CSVs, commit each to the repo root under a fixed filename, overwriting yesterday's copy so there is always exactly one current copy of each:

| Repo file (fixed) | Local CSV |
|---|---|
| events.csv | events.csv |
| parks.csv | parks.csv |
| deals.csv | deals.csv |
| volunteer.csv | volunteer.csv |
| restaurants.csv | restaurants.csv |

For each file: base64-encode the local CSV's raw bytes; GET `https://api.github.com/repos/avlhohn/msp-family-feed/contents/<file>?ref=main` to read the current `sha` (if that GET returns `404`, omit `sha` and create it fresh); then PUT to the same URL with body `{"message": "Daily <file> <TODAY>", "content": "<base64>", "branch": "main", "sha": "<sha>"}` and the same headers as the feed PUT below (`Authorization: Bearer <token>`, `Accept: application/vnd.github+json`, `X-GitHub-Api-Version: 2022-11-28`, `User-Agent: msp-family-feed`). A `200`/`201` response means the commit landed. These CSVs are the human-facing archive that used to live in Drive — GitHub renders CSVs as sortable tables, so they can be browsed directly in the repo. The old descriptive sheet titles with embedded date ranges (e.g. "Events ({TODAY} to {END_DATE})") are dropped in favor of these stable filenames; the dates live in the rows and in the feed.

**If any of the 5 CSV commits fails** (tool error, non-2xx response, timeout), retry once; if it still fails, log it as an `error`-severity `pipeline`-category row in STEP 7's error log (issue_type `sheet_publish_failure`, item = which CSV) — do not let a failed publish silently drop a table without a record of why. Do NOT fall back to uploading it to Drive; the Drive sheets are retired.

**Publish the feed to GitHub (this is the delivery path Base44 reads — it REPLACES the old Google Drive feed-folder upload):** after STEP 5 has written the local `msp_family_guide.json`, publish that exact file to the public GitHub repo **`avlhohn/msp-family-feed`**, path **`msp_family_guide.json`**, branch **`main`**, via the GitHub contents API. This final enriched copy supersedes the STEP 4.4 checkpoint. Publishing to GitHub is what makes the feed reliably available to Base44 at a stable public URL with NO Google Drive dependency and NO machine-awake dependency. **Do NOT upload the feed JSON to Google Drive anymore** — the feed JSON's one and only delivery path is now GitHub, and the 5 category tables above are likewise GitHub-only now.

- **Auth (token, never exposed) — fetch it from Google Drive at runtime:** the fine-grained GitHub token is stored in a private Google Drive file named exactly **`msp_feed_gh_token.txt`** (it lives in the archive folder `1SlAJxG1H-T1-Dnsoy2HYmuOfOTZLmGwX`, file id `12-gYyeDisDch9Crw-zdeloD9eabY363x`). Do NOT expect a `github_token.txt` sitting in the working folder — a headless scheduled run executes in an isolated session that CANNOT see files placed in the working folder, so the token MUST be pulled from Drive each run. Resolve it like this:
  1. Locate the file: `mcp__d732aa5d-aafa-4b34-be2c-9272ed01e5d4__search_files` with query `title = 'msp_feed_gh_token.txt'` (or read the known file id above directly).
  2. Download it with `mcp__d732aa5d-aafa-4b34-be2c-9272ed01e5d4__download_file_content` — this returns the raw bytes **base64-encoded**. base64-decode that and `.strip()` to get the token. **Use `download_file_content`, NOT `read_file_content`** — `read_file_content` returns a "natural language" rendering that escapes every underscore as `\_`, which corrupts the token (a GitHub PAT contains underscores). If you must use `read_file_content`, remove all backslashes before use.
  3. Write the decoded token to `github_token.txt` in the current session's working folder (so the `gh_publish_feed.py` helper can pick it up), OR hold it in memory and pass it straight into the `Authorization: Bearer <token>` header.
  Use the token ONLY as an HTTP `Authorization: Bearer <token>` header — never print it, never echo it to stdout, never write it to any log, never commit it into a file (writing it to the local `github_token.txt` scratch copy is fine; that file is not committed and does not persist). If the Drive token file can't be found, is empty, or fails to decode, do NOT fail silently: log an `error`-severity `pipeline` row (issue_type `feed_delivery_failed`, item `missing_github_token`) and skip the publish (the run's other outputs still complete).
- **Upload:** base64-encode the local JSON's raw bytes. First GET `https://api.github.com/repos/avlhohn/msp-family-feed/contents/msp_family_guide.json?ref=main` to read the current file's `sha` (the file already exists, so a `sha` is required to update it; if that GET returns `404`, omit `sha` and create it fresh). Then PUT to the same URL with JSON body `{"message": "Daily feed <TODAY>", "content": "<base64>", "branch": "main", "sha": "<sha>"}` and headers `Authorization: Bearer <token>`, `Accept: application/vnd.github+json`, `X-GitHub-Api-Version: 2022-11-28`, `User-Agent: msp-family-feed`. A `200`/`201` response means the commit landed. (A round-trip integrity test confirmed this API preserves the full ~385KB JSON byte-for-byte.)
- **Verify-and-repair loop (required):** immediately after the PUT, GET `https://api.github.com/repos/avlhohn/msp-family-feed/contents/msp_family_guide.json?ref=main` again — this API endpoint is NOT CDN-cached (unlike the raw URL), so it reflects the commit instantly — and confirm BOTH: (1) its `size` is at least ~50,000 bytes (a real feed is hundreds of KB; STEP 8's record count should be in the hundreds — under ~10 KB is a corrupted/stub upload), and (2) the returned `sha` is DIFFERENT from the pre-upload `sha` you read (proof your commit replaced the old file). If either check fails, re-run the GET-sha + PUT cycle. Attempt the upload+verify cycle up to **3 times total**. On each failed attempt that triggered a repair, log a `warning`-severity `pipeline` row (issue_type `feed_repair`, item = which check failed). If all 3 attempts fail, log an `error`-severity `pipeline` row (issue_type `feed_delivery_failed`) with the last-seen state (size + sha) — this is a hard failure worth surfacing, since it means Base44 may be serving a stale feed.
- **How Base44 reads it:** Base44's `dailyJsonImport` pulls the feed from the public raw URL `https://raw.githubusercontent.com/avlhohn/msp-family-feed/main/msp_family_guide.json` on its own schedule; a public repo needs NO token to read, so there is nothing on the read side that can silently expire. Raw URLs can be CDN-cached for a few minutes, which is harmless for a once-daily feed — the committed file is the source of truth.
- **Also re-publish the Wikimedia cache `_wiki_cache.json` (added 2026-08-29):** after STEP 4.8 runs, the working folder holds a freshened `_wiki_cache.json` (the venue-lookup cache the layer self-primed at startup, then extended with any new lookups). Commit it back to the repo `avlhohn/msp-family-feed`, path `_wiki_cache.json`, branch `main`, using the exact same GET-sha → PUT → confirm-200/201 mechanics as the CSV publish above (reuse the token). This is what closes the warm-start loop: STEP 4.8's helper fetches this file at the *start* of the next run so a cold sandbox lands ~50 upgrades in one pass instead of ~41. It is NOT app-critical (Base44 never reads it), so treat a failure as low-severity: retry once, then log a `warning` `pipeline` row (issue_type `wiki_cache_publish_failed`) and carry on — the feed and CSVs still publish. `publish_feed.py` ships it directly: `python3 publish_feed.py "Update Wikimedia venue cache <TODAY>" --only _wiki_cache.json`. Skip this only if STEP 4.8 itself was skipped (no cache was produced).
- A ready-to-use publisher helper, `gh_publish_feed.py`, may live in this task's working folder (it reads the token from a local `github_token.txt`, does the GET-sha → PUT → verify loop). **Because a headless run cannot see working-folder files, do NOT rely on the helper being present** — after decoding the Drive token in the Auth bullet above, write it to `github_token.txt` in the working folder, and if `gh_publish_feed.py` is also absent, just implement the GET-sha → PUT → verify loop inline per the bullets above. When present, run it as `python3 gh_publish_feed.py <path-to-msp_family_guide.json>`.

## STEP 6.5 — Facebook shadow trial (DORMANT — superseded by the live STEP 4 pull on 2026-08-22)

**⚠️ DO NOT RUN THIS STEP.** `RUN_FB_SHADOW` is hard-set to False in STEP 1 (superseded 2026-08-22). The Facebook scraper now runs LIVE and folds its filtered results into the feed via STEP 4, so this read-only trial is no longer needed — and running it on a Monday would call the paid Apify actor a SECOND time and **double the run's spend**. On every run, skip this step: log a single `pipeline` `info` row (issue_type `fb_shadow_skip`, item `superseded_by_step4`) and proceed to STEP 7. The prose below is retained ONLY as a reference for the filter logic that STEP 4 now uses; it is not an active step.

**Purpose (historical):** measure what the Apify Facebook Events scraper *would* bring in — across ALL areas we tag — so the user could judge its cost vs. value before wiring it into the live feed. That decision was made on 2026-08-22 (turn it on, weekly), so this evaluation is complete. The classify/date-window/geo-guard/host-type/denylist/net-new logic below is now applied for real in STEP 4, where survivors are KEPT into `events` instead of merely logged.

**Run gate:** DORMANT — do not run regardless of the day. (`RUN_FB_SHADOW` is False in STEP 1.) The remainder of this section is reference material only.

**How to run it (Mondays only):**
1. Call the connected Apify **`apify/facebook-events-scraper`** actor (via the Apify MCP — NOT WebFetch/bash) with `maxEvents` = **`APIFY_MAX_EVENTS` (280 — the STEP 1 cost cap; a single TOTAL cap for the whole run, NOT per query)** and `searchQueries` covering ALL areas we tag:
   `["Minneapolis Minnesota family", "St Paul Minnesota family", "Duluth Minnesota family", "Rochester Minnesota family", "St Cloud Minnesota kids", "Mankato Minnesota family", "Moorhead Minnesota family", "Brainerd Minnesota family", "Bemidji Minnesota family", "Winona Minnesota family", "Alexandria Minnesota family", "Grand Rapids Minnesota family"]`.
   **Cost cap (hard):** you MUST pass `maxEvents = 280` and MUST NOT set it per-query or leave it empty (empty = unlimited = unbounded cost). At $0.013/event this bounds the run at ~$3.64, under the $4.00 `APIFY_MAX_USD` limit. The 12 queries share this single 280-event budget (~23 events/region), which is a fine sample for a weekly probe.
   Then read the run's dataset items. **Resilience:** if the actor errors, times out, is not connected, or returns nothing, log a `pipeline` `info` row (issue_type `fb_shadow_skip`, item = the reason) and carry on — NEVER a hard failure, and never let this delay or block STEP 7/8.
   **Post-run cost check:** after the run reaches a terminal state, call `get-actor-run` and read the actual events-extracted count (and charge, if the run stats expose it). Compute est. cost = events_extracted × $0.013. If that est. cost exceeds `APIFY_MAX_USD` (4.00) — which should be impossible with `maxEvents = 280`, but guards against a pricing change or the actor ignoring the cap — log a `warning`-severity `pipeline` row (issue_type `fb_shadow_cost_exceeded`, item = the est. cost + event count) so an overspend is caught immediately, and drop `APIFY_MAX_EVENTS` for the next run.
2. **Classify every returned item (do NOT discard — in shadow mode we record the verdict for each so the user can see what the filter is doing).** For each item compute a `verdict` of `kept` or `dropped` plus a `drop_reason`, applying these filters in order:
   - **Date window:** `dropped`/`past_or_out_of_window` if `isPast: true` or start date is outside `[TODAY, END_DATE]`.
   - **MN-only geo guard:** `dropped`/`out_of_state` if `location` city/name resolves to any non-MN state. Record the resolved city + state in the log columns. Apply the known traps explicitly: "Grand Rapids" pulls Grand Rapids **Michigan**; "Winona" pulls Winona in **GA/MS/other states**; "Twin Cities"/metro queries have pulled Saginaw MI, Sioux City IA, Spokane WA. Any non-MN resolve is `out_of_state`.
   - **Host-type filter (the primary private-event guard):** determine whether the event is hosted by a Facebook **Page** (venue, museum, library, park, city, business, org, public figure) or by an **individual personal profile**. Record `host_name` and `host_type` (`page` / `personal` / `unknown`). Genuinely private/invite-only events do not surface in anonymous public search, so the real risk is public-but-personal items — those are almost always personal-profile-hosted. Set `verdict = dropped`, `drop_reason = personal_host` for personal-profile hosts. If host type can't be determined, set `host_type = unknown` and keep it but mark `drop_reason = host_unknown` in a way that's visibly flagged (so unknowns are never silently treated as clean).
   - **Denylist (keep it `dropped`/`denylist_<term>`):** garage/estate/rummage/moving/"multi-family" sales, truckload/produce/meat fundraiser sales, gun shows, private "members/family only" reunions, birthday/wedding/funeral/celebration-of-life, MLM/vendor-recruitment/"try it" pitches, adult-only/21+/bar-crawl events.
   - Everything that survives all of the above is `verdict = kept`. Note positive signals (festival, storytime, library, museum, nature center, planetarium, park program, fair, parade, all-ages concert, family fun day, touch-a-truck, fireworks) in a `positive_signal` column to help the user eyeball quality.
3. **Net-new detection (the key value signal):** for each `kept` item, check whether the built `events` already contains the same event — same/similar title + same date + same or nearby venue (reuse the STEP 4.3 duplicate-matching judgment). Set `net_new = Y` if `events` does NOT already have it, `net_new = N` (and record `matched_existing_title`) if it does. This distinguishes events Facebook would ADD from ones our calendar/CVB sources already cover.
4. **Write the side log — `fb_shadow_log.csv` — and publish it (this is the ONLY output):** append this run's rows to the carried-forward `fb_shadow_log.csv` so weekly data accumulates over the trial. Load the base the same way as the error log (local `mnt/Agents and Workflows/fb_shadow_log.csv` if present, else fetch `https://raw.githubusercontent.com/avlhohn/msp-family-feed/main/fb_shadow_log.csv`, else start fresh if 404). Columns (15): `run_date, query_region, event_name, start_date, host_name, host_type, location_city, location_state, mn_ok, verdict, drop_reason, positive_signal, net_new, matched_existing_title, url`. Then publish it to the repo `avlhohn/msp-family-feed`, path `fb_shadow_log.csv`, branch `main`, using the exact same GitHub contents-API mechanics as the STEP 6 CSV publish (reuse the token; GET sha → PUT → confirm 200/201). If the publish fails, retry once, then log a `warning` `pipeline` row (issue_type `fb_shadow_publish_failed`) — do not block the run. **Guardrail:** `fb_shadow_log.csv` is the ONLY file this step writes. Under no circumstances fold any of these items into `events`/`events.csv`/`msp_family_guide.json` or any other category — the trial is strictly observational.

**Contribution/yield log (STEP 7):** log one `pipeline` `info` row (issue_type `fb_shadow_yield`) summarizing the run: total pulled, total kept, dropped split by reason (`past_or_out_of_window` / `out_of_state` / `personal_host` / `denylist`), and of the kept how many were `net_new` vs. already-covered. This is the running record of whether the scraper is earning its per-run cost.

---

## STEP 7 — Error & diagnostics log

Maintain a single running, append-only diagnostics log so issues can be reviewed and diagnosed across days without digging through run transcripts. This log is also the input queue for the companion `msp-family-guide-error-fixer` task (runs daily, shortly after this one) — treat `unresolved_website` and `unresolved_image` rows as items that WILL be re-attempted automatically, not just passive records.

**Schema (10 fields):** `run_date, severity, category, step, issue_type, item, description, resolved_date, resolved_by, resolution_note`
- `severity`: `error` (hard tool/upload/script failures), `warning` (data-quality issues like low hit-rates, rate limits, notable count drops), or `info` (unresolved items left blank after a genuine attempt, e.g. STEP 4.6/4.5 misses)
- `category`: `events`, `parks`, `meal_deals`, `volunteer_opportunities`, `restaurants`, or `pipeline` (for run-wide/cross-cutting issues not tied to one category)
- `step`: which step the issue occurred in (e.g. `STEP4_apify`, `STEP4.5_image_backfill`, `STEP4.6_website_backfill`, `STEP5_build`, `STEP6_github_publish`, `STEP6.5_fb_shadow`, `STEP3_dedup`)
- `issue_type`: short machine-readable tag, e.g. `tool_failure`, `rate_limit`, `low_hit_rate`, `count_drop`, `unresolved_website`, `unresolved_image`, `build_failure`, `sheet_publish_failure`, `log_publish_failed`, `drive_upload_failure`, `duplicate_flagged`, `stock_unavailable`, `image_upgrade_skipped`, `curated_stale`, `curated_bad_url`, `feed_repair`, `feed_delivery_failed`, `feed_stray_copy`, `feed_checkpoint`, `seed_venue_missing`, `play_cafe_low`, `dropped_closed_venues`, `fb_pull_skip`, `fb_pull_zero`, `fb_pull_cost`, `fb_pull_cost_exceeded`, `fb_pull_yield`, `fb_shadow_skip` (now only ever `superseded_by_step4`), `run_summary`
- `item`: the specific item title if the issue is about one item; blank for run-wide issues
- `description`: one to two sentence free-text explanation, specific enough to act on without re-deriving context
- `resolved_date`, `resolved_by`, `resolution_note`: always leave these 3 columns BLANK when logging a new row — this task never resolves issues itself. They are populated exclusively by the `msp-family-guide-error-fixer` task when it later confirms a fix. Never delete or blank out a row that already has these filled in from a prior fixer run — preserve resolved rows as history, don't re-open them.

**Deduplication (required):** before adding a new `unresolved_website` or `unresolved_image` row for a given item, check whether an OPEN row (same `item` + same `issue_type`, `resolved_date` still blank) already exists in the carried-forward log. If so, skip — don't add a duplicate. Only add a fresh row for a genuinely new issue (a new item, or an item whose previously-resolved issue has recurred).

**Every run must log at least one row** — a `run_summary` info row with record counts and a one-line rollup of how many errors/warnings/info items follow, even on a totally clean run with zero other issues. This makes it obvious the log itself is alive and wasn't skipped, rather than looking identical to "log broke and produced nothing."

**Carry-forward (evergreen — the canonical error log now lives on GitHub, not Drive):** the shared `error_log.csv` is stored in the public GitHub repo `avlhohn/msp-family-feed` (path `error_log.csv`, branch `main`). This is the single handoff file shared with the `msp-family-guide-error-fixer` task — the build appends new issues to it, the fixer resolves rows in it, and each run carries the other's work forward. Load the base in this priority order, never discarding prior days' rows (including ones already marked resolved by the fixer task):
  1. A local `error_log.csv` in the current session's `mnt/Agents and Workflows/` folder, if present.
  2. Else fetch the canonical copy from GitHub: `https://raw.githubusercontent.com/avlhohn/msp-family-feed/main/error_log.csv` (a public raw URL, no token needed to read).
  3. **One-time migration fallback:** if the GitHub fetch returns 404 (the log hasn't been seeded to GitHub yet on the very first run after this change), search Drive folder `1XA7M_7oFGPxtY3fqHVxueXFwkM3azrLo` ("Error Fixing - Latest") for the most recently created readable `error_log.csv` and use that as the base, so the full accumulated history carries over into GitHub. After this first successful GitHub publish, step 2 will always succeed and this Drive fallback won't fire again.

**Validate the base before using it (guards against a truncated/wrong-schema file poisoning the carry-forward — this has happened before):** whatever base you load (local, GitHub, or the one-time Drive fallback), confirm BOTH (a) its header is exactly the expected 10-column schema `run_date, severity, category, step, issue_type, item, description, resolved_date, resolved_by, resolution_note`, and (b) its row count is plausible — the log grows monotonically over time, so it should have at least roughly as many rows as recent days' copies, never a sudden collapse to a handful of rows. If the loaded base fails either check (different/short header, or far fewer rows than expected — e.g. a stray sub-1KB file with a different schema), do NOT use it as the base: if you were on the Drive one-time-migration fallback, fall through to the next most-recently-created readable `error_log.csv` that passes both checks; log a `warning`-severity `pipeline` row (issue_type `log_base_rejected`) naming the rejected source and why. Never overwrite good accumulated history with a truncated base.

**Write outputs:**
1. Append today's new rows to the carried-forward content and write the result to the current session's `mnt/Agents and Workflows/error_log.csv`.
2. **Publish the full updated `error_log.csv` to GitHub — overwrite in place** (this REPLACES the old Drive-folder upload; it's the shared handoff so the newly-logged issues reach the `msp-family-guide-error-fixer` task). Commit it to the repo `avlhohn/msp-family-feed`, path `error_log.csv`, branch `main`, using the same GitHub contents-API mechanics as the STEP 6 CSV publish: reuse the token already fetched in STEP 6 (the `msp_feed_gh_token.txt` Drive file); GET `https://api.github.com/repos/avlhohn/msp-family-feed/contents/error_log.csv?ref=main` for the current `sha` (omit `sha` if 404 — first seed), then PUT `{"message": "Error log <TODAY>", "content": "<base64>", "branch": "main", "sha": "<sha>"}` with headers `Authorization: Bearer <token>`, `Accept: application/vnd.github+json`, `X-GitHub-Api-Version: 2022-11-28`, `User-Agent: msp-family-feed`. Treat this as a required step. **Verify:** after the PUT, GET the contents API again and confirm the returned `size` is at least ~10,000 bytes (the log runs ~60 KB; a tiny size means a truncated upload) AND the `sha` changed from the pre-upload value; if either check fails, retry the GET-sha + PUT cycle up to 3 times total. If all 3 fail, log an `error`-severity `pipeline` row (issue_type `log_publish_failed`) — do NOT fall back to Drive; the Drive error-log folder is retired as the handoff path. Do not convert or upload to Google Drive.

---

## STEP 8 — Output summary

Print a summary table:
- Record counts per category
- Within `events`: how many are sports/active-rec items and how many are paid activities/classes/camps this run (count by the `sports`/`paid`/`class`/`camp` tags), so the sports & paid-opportunity expansion is visibly tracked over time; flag if either falls below the ~15-item target
- Within `restaurants`: total count and how many are kid-experience venues (count by the dining experience tags — `play-area`/`arcade`/`brewery-patio`/`character-dining`/`themed`, etc.) vs. deal-only, so the family-dining expansion is visibly tracked; flag if the total falls below the ~25-item target
- Within `events`: how many are community/special-interest items (count by the `car-show`/`cruise-night`/`parade`/`air-show`/`community-fest`/`touch-a-truck`/`craft-fair`/`food-truck`/`festival` tags), so the community-events expansion is visibly tracked; flag if this comes back near zero (would indicate the calendar-aggregator sources and non-keyword searches aren't landing)
- Early-childhood & establishment coverage: how many items (across `events` + `restaurants`) carry the establishment tags (`play-cafe`/`indoor-playground`/`tot-time`/`story-time`/`open-play`/`music-class`/`kids-gym`/`ecfe`), and the age-band breakdown of the whole dataset by age-group tag (`babies`/`toddlers`/`preschool`/`kids`/`tweens`/`teens`), so the youngest/oldest-band coverage is visibly tracked; flag if `babies`/`toddlers` or `teens` come back near zero. Also confirm every venue on the **must-include local venues** seed list (STEP 2) is present in the output — if any is absent (and was not logged as `dropped_closed_venues`), log a `warning`-severity `pipeline` row (issue_type `seed_venue_missing`) naming it, since seed venues are guaranteed inclusions
- **Play-café coverage check (specific user priority — don't let this niche silently disappear):** count the items tagged `play-cafe` specifically, and separately the count carrying `play-cafe` OR `play-area`. The STEP 2 must-include seed list alone guarantees ~15+ play/kid-café venues, so the `play-cafe`-tagged count should land in at least the low-double-digits. If the `play-cafe` count comes back **below ~12** (or near zero), that means the seed venues didn't get emitted/tagged and/or the café-with-play-space searches didn't land — log a `warning`-severity `pipeline` row (issue_type `play_cafe_low`) with the actual count and which seed venues were missing, so the shortfall is caught automatically instead of by eye. This is separate from `seed_venue_missing` (which names individual absent venues); `play_cafe_low` is the aggregate tripwire for the whole category.
- How many items in each category have `image_url` populated — broken down by `image_source` (`facebook` vs. `og_image` vs. `site_photo` vs. `openverse_named` vs. `stock_openverse` vs. `curated` vs. `curated_category` vs. blank/other) — vs. empty
- **Facebook weekly pull (STEP 4 — Mondays only):** if it ran this run (`RUN_APIFY` True, i.e. a Monday), report the numbers so the cost/value picture is visible over time: total items pulled, **estimated Apify cost this run** (events_extracted × $0.013, which must be ≤ $5.00 — flag loudly if not), total kept after filtering, the drop breakdown (`past_or_out_of_window` / `out_of_state` / `personal_host` / `host_unknown` / `denylist`), and of the kept how many are **net-new** (not already in `events` before STEP 4) vs. duplicates STEP 4.3 collapsed. If it did NOT run (any non-Monday), state "Facebook pull skipped (not Monday)". Emphasize the **net-new count against the per-run Apify cost** — that is the number that shows whether the weekly scraper is earning its keep. Unlike the old shadow trial, these KEPT items DID enter the live feed this run (with `image_source: facebook`).
- How many og:image candidates were demoted this run as generic homepage/aggregator/umbrella banners (STEP 4.5 relevance filter) and thus routed to a stock image instead
- How many items received a `stock_openverse` fallback image this run (STEP 4.7), and how many distinct stock URLs were used across them (a proxy for image variety)
- How many items were upgraded to `openverse_named` (real photo of the named place), how many to `curated` (marquee title/keyword override), and how many to `curated_category` (topical category/tag fallback) this run in STEP 4.8. For `curated_category`, also note the breakdown of how many rode a specific `tag` image vs. the coarser `category` image — a high `category`-only count signals which tags are still missing a fallback image and would benefit from being added to `curated_images.csv`
- How many items in each category have `website` populated (and via which source: original research vs. STEP 4.6 backfill) vs. left blank, and why (ambiguous/closed/rebranded/no confident match)
- **Precedence & de-duplication (STEP 4.3):** total `duplicate_flagged` merges this run, split into how many were **seed overrides** (a must-include seed row winning over a scraped duplicate) vs. **scrape-vs-scrape** collapses, and — for parks specifically — any park entries merged (old name(s) → merged name). A healthy run typically shows a handful of seed overrides (the seed venues that broad search also happened to find); zero seed overrides across many runs would hint the seed rows aren't being emitted before the merge
- Any legacy park rows retroactively tagged with `image_source` this run (per the STEP 3 note), and how many
- **Error/diagnostics log summary:** how many new rows were added this run, broken down by severity (error/warning/info), and a one-line callout of anything `error`-severity (hard failures) so those are never buried
- The GitHub commit/URL for each of the 5 category CSVs (`https://github.com/avlhohn/msp-family-feed/blob/main/<file>`), plus the `error_log.csv` GitHub URL (`https://github.com/avlhohn/msp-family-feed/blob/main/error_log.csv`)
- The `msp_family_guide.json` GitHub raw URL (`https://raw.githubusercontent.com/avlhohn/msp-family-feed/main/msp_family_guide.json`, published in STEP 6) AND its local path — the feed is no longer uploaded to Google Drive
- Run parameters (RUN_APIFY status, RUN_FB_SHADOW status, date range, day of week)
- Any cities where event count was notably low (< 3 events found)
- Any significant drop in the parks count vs. the prior evergreen list (if the carried-forward list is smaller than before, flag it explicitly rather than treating it as normal churn)
